Despite his efforts, it was visible that he was experiencing discomfort even with the not-so-subtle stares Devi shot at her husband to get ahold of himself for the sake of his daughter.
Seeing his son’s future father-in-law break out into a sweat, Ramdev said, “Brother, we can absolutely understand that this is very short notice. Vishnu is a busy city business man, and planning around this auspicious day with him having only a few holidays, has been tricky on our end. Given the short time frame, we think it best that these two start their married journey soon. And as to the big question of dowry, let me reassure you, we have no expectation to receive anything from your family. We are a modern family and we are already taking your most precious possession, your lovely daughter, Lakshmi.”
Devi and her husband’s jaws dropped in surprise at the suggestion. They looked at each other in disbelief and then at their daughter’s face, then back to each other. They couldn’t believe the good news they were hearing. They both thought to themselves, “This girl is truly our lucky charm. How incredible.”
“Brother, you have no idea how relieved we are. But if there is anything, please, please, do not hesitate to let us know what we can do,” Lakshmi’s father went on to reply with his hands in a namaste position and with his head bowed down in deep respect to Ramdev, Gayatri, the village elder and the remaining family members. “We are ever so grateful for your kindness.”
“There is one condition to the marriage, however,” Gayatri interrupted Arvind. In that moment, their hearts dropped as Lakshmi and her parents felt numb as their fears returned. “Our sweet boy, Vishnu, loved Lakshmi’s special cardamom tea. It was simply delicious, however, that won’t be enough to satisfy
his needs in Kochi. Lakshmi, for the next few weeks as you get ready for the wedding, you will need to learn to cook for my son the way he likes his food prepared. Cook and I will supervise your training and it will be best you leave your job at the cardamom estate immediately and come stay with us for that time.”
This request was highly unusual and unexpected. Devi and her husband hesitantly nodded in acceptance. It wasn’t heard of that a future daughter-in-law would stay at her in-laws’ home before marriage. They didn’t want Lakshmi’s honor to be tainted if anything happened during that time. They didn’t want to say anything in case that would further jeopardize Lakshmi’s chances.
Realizing the concern from Lakshmi’s parents’ faces, Gayatri then said, “Okay, okay. I am sure you want all the time with your daughter before she marries our son and goes off to live in her 3 BHK in Kochi. How about we have our driver pick her up every day and drop her off every evening. But now with one month left before the wedding, however, she will have to stop working at the cardamom farms. She is our daughter now and cannot be seen working at a farm.”
This compromise was appealing to Devi and her husband. They were overjoyed by the kindness and insightfulness of Ramdev and Gayatri. Their daughter was set to marry the wealthiest family in their village in the outskirts of Idukki and her journey would take her directly to a luxurious waterfront home on Marine Drive in the big city. The parents were more than ecstatic, but also wondered why their daughter would need to learn to cook when they would have a maid in Kochi to do so. They dismissed this, rationalizing that every real Indian woman should be able to prepare meals for her husband.
The evening wrapped up with a lavish dinner of delicious Indian vegetarian food, but also an unusual dish. To be polite, Devi’s father was about to dish out a bit for himself, but Ramdev stopped him.
“Brother, no, no. This is specially made only for Vishnu. He knows better, but living in Kochi, he has developed a liking for seafood,” Ramdev said seeing the perplexed face of Lakshmi’s father. “Don’t worry, Cook and Gayatri will teach Lakshmi all she needs to know to make this. She must adjust to the higher society in Kochi and learn dishes like this Kerala fish fry.”
Although taken aback by Vishnu’s sacrilegious diet, Lakshmi’s parents understood that Vishnu was of an elite class and his ways would, of course, be different as he was an esteemed businessman in the big city. They had watched in the Tollywood films and understood that their village ways were far different from those of the less conservative, cosmopolitan city folk. They had to accept that their daughter would have to make these small sacrifices for the sake of a very comfortable future.
After dinner, they retired to the living area of this grand room. It felt like the Taj Mahal in comparison to their small dwellings. Gayatri requested Lakshmi, her future daughter-in-law, to make her special cardamom tea for everyone. Between sips and pecks at delicious butter cookies, they discussed the wedding ceremonies that we going to take place over four days. Gayatri took charge as this was her area of expertise. She told Ramdev that this would be an extravagant affair as Vishnu was her only son and she had one chance for a grand, memorable gesture that the villagers would talk about for years to come. Ramdev was more than happy to step aside and only focus on the finance aspect. From his perspective, he too wanted a lavish wedding to rub his brother Ravi’s nose in it.
Given the wealth dynamics and the lack of dowry demands, Lakshmi’s family really didn’t have much input and were fine to oblige. Having the burden of the dowry off the table, they could breathe much more easily and were enjoying the conversations much more now.
The engagement was simple and did not involve a lot of pomp and circumstance. After the tea and butter cookies, Gayatri presented two solid 24K rings. Lakshmi and Vishnu had largely been silent as their parents discussed matters around their wedding and when the plans seemed to solidify, Gayatri ushered the two to exchange rings.
This would be the first time Lakshmi would feel Vishnu’s touch. She took a deep breath and held it as he took her hand in hers and put the ring on her ring finger. Lakshmi’s cheeks went a blush pink, matching the sari’s color she adorned and she demurely smiled. As he held her hand, she felt a spark throughout her body as it came alive. She felt a happiness she had never felt before and could not make sense of the feeling. She couldn’t wait to tell Rani all about her engagement and this unusual sensation she felt. Next, Lakshmi took Vishnu’s ring that Gayatri had so graciously provided, and placed it on Vishnu’s ring finger. And with that, Lakshmi and Vishnu were engaged. The two families rejoiced over sweets and had Cook take multiple pictures of the family to memorialize this joyous moment.
~
The village was buzzing with the wedding news, which had spread like wild fire. Trisha and Rani had told their close friends. Then those close friends told theirs, and from there everyone started telling theirs until the full village knew. It had even reached the ears of Kanika madam and her family. And then,
the best news for all. The whole village had been invited to the wedding, which the villagers knew was sure to be a lavish affair.
The first work day after the engagement, Lakshmi and her mother took a break to privately speak with Kanika madam and her mother with dual intentions. First, they wanted to informally extend a wedding invite to their family more as a gesture, knowing that such elite were unlikely to attend a villagers’ wedding. Even the village elder and his family stood far below status in comparison to Kanika madam and her family. They had unbelievable old money wealth that would last generations and could feed the nation. Secondly, they wanted to tell them that this would be Lakshmi’s last day of service.
“Oh wow, Lakshmi. Congratulations! Thank you for the invite,” Kanika madam said and surprised Lakshmi with a warm embrace.
“Yes, and we understand that you must leave us now. So many young village girls I’ve seen married off, but never any moving to Kochi. Just know that you and your mother will always have a place with us if you ever wish to return. We have known your family for a very long time,” Kanika’s mother said.
Lakshmi and her mother didn’t stay too long as not to inconvenience them, but were very happy from their response. They profusely thanked their employers and let them know how gracious they were as the wedding had materialized so quickly. Before leaving, Devi again insisted that they attend their wedding, more so out of obligation and formality than anything else.
~
When they left, Kanika heard her parents talk, well more her mother chattering away at her father. “These poor girls get
married so young, but what other future does a girl like her have. Lakshmi’s family is lucky though that they didn’t ask for a dowry. Very, very lucky. I was afraid they had come here to beg for help from us, for a loan they’ll never pay back. At least one less burden for that family,” Kanika madam overheard her mother say.
Thinking about that, Kanika thought how lucky Lakshmi was to meet her prince charming at such a young age and be empowered to build her own family life. She too longed to escape as she was so tired of being her mother’s barbie doll and to discover herself away from her.
Her mother had always been strict with her, while her brothers had free range. She never scolded them for all the stupid boy things they’d do. “Boy will be boys,” Kanika’s mother would say. On the other hand, Kanika had to keep a regimented lifestyle, because God forbid, she gained an ounce before the next soiree and embarrass her mother in front of her mother’s friends. It was so tiring to keep her size XS figure that wasn’t naturally hers to keep. She had a nutritionist and physical trainer who saw that every calorie and sweat bead contributed to maintaining that model thin physique that her mother could show off to her friends with.
“Palavi, when I was her age, I was even thinner than Kanika,” her mother would say, although she was now plump all around, which she blamed Kanika for as it was during the pregnancy she was carrying her that she put on that weight.
Kanika did everything to please this woman, but nothing was ever enough. During the tea afternoons, she would pretend that she wasn’t hungry and would sip her cardamom tea with no sugar although she absolutely loved eating all kinds of sweets. It was exhausting being the only daughter of such a wealthy family and ensuring that she maintained her grace and beauty.
Kanika sat there pondering what her life would be like if she too had full reign of her own household and got to eat whatever she wanted, whenever she wanted. She could let down her hair, stay out in the sun without fear that her skin would darken and become undesirable, and allow her nails to grow without the mandatory weekly manicures. She couldn’t wait to be married off and live her life, her way.
Lounging on her four-poster bed, she smiled so largely in defiance of her mother and felt pure joy. As she lay her head on the down feather stuffed pillow covered in a soft pink silk covering, she continued to smile pushing her abnormally large front teeth out. “Yes, mother. I will be able to smile like this without you harassing me that I need to practice a smaller smile. So I will look demurer and more attractive. I will find a man that will find all my awkwardnesses, attractive. Just you wait,” Kanika whispered as she slowly drifted off to sleep in her large, air-conditioned room.
~
Lakshmi was enjoying the luxury of being an engaged woman and the attention she was being showered with. Some villagers were genuinely excited for the upcoming event of the year. Others were, of course, not so happy and secretly prayed that somehow the wedding would be a bust. They took pleasure in the anticipated disgrace that would befall Lakshmi’s family.
Lakshmi, however, remained naïve to the negativity around her and was enjoying the bliss of being a bride-to-be. For the first time in her life, she no longer had to worry about fetching water from the well in the early hours of the morning. In fact, she was now forbidden to even enter her mother’s kitchen. She was prescribed full rest and that morning, she was to head up to her future in-laws’ home for her “marriage training course.”
She couldn’t sit around that morning waiting to be picked up, so she ventured out into the Gangalee fields where she lay down on a soft grassy area. She looked up to the clear sky with the sun gently kissing her skin. Her mother and Trisha would scold her for lying there now and allowing the sun’s rays to sully her glowing, fair skin, but she felt so much peace as she inhaled the fresh air surrounding her that was gently scented with the perfume of the Gangalee blossoms.
In her contentment she must have lost track of time because she suddenly heard Rani call out to her, “Lakshmi, Lakshmi, wake up. Hurry. The driver is here to pick you up. We’ve been looking everywhere for you. Let’s go. You’re late,” Rani yelled as she grabbed Lakshmi’s hand and lifted her up. The two girls sprinted back to Lakshmi’s home.
As it would happen, her mother was extremely angry at Lakshmi’s tardiness, especially given the magnitude of the situation. She couldn’t believe how lightly Lakshmi was taking this and had gone out day dreaming at such a critical time for her future.
“You may be engaged, but you cannot get lazy now. They can still toss you aside, silly girl. How can you be late on your first day? How?” Devi hollered angrily at her daughter.
Quickly, Lakshmi changed into her clothes and freshened up. They were running half an hour behind schedule already. Her mother appeased the driver with a cup of cardamom tea and tried to have him come inside and take a seat as he waiting by the car. He humbly declined to come in but took the tea.
“Even for him, our house must seem meager. He has his own quarters near that grand house of the village provided by them,” Devi thought. Devi stalled the driver as long as she could, but finally Lakshmi was ready and ran out the door. Devi and Rani
wished her the best of luck and prayed that Vishnu’s family would be forgiving. Her tardiness was certainly not a good first impression and could jeopardize this opportunity.
~
Gayatri was a stickler for punctuality and was angrily pacing back and forth outside the home, furious at Lakshmi’s lateness. She was a woman who viewed being on time as the cornerstone of etiquette. She looked at the clock and with every minute passing, grew more impatient.
“These classless village girls. Now that she’s engaged, she thinks she can do whatever the hell she likes,” Gayatri shook her head in disappointment. “Cook, Aaya, any sign of the car yet?” she yelled. When the answer was no, she grew more irate and restless. Her son was her golden boy who deserved a wife that understood the importance of regiment. She would need to set Lakshmi straight about expectations when she came. Her tardiness was inexcusable.
~
Finally, the car drove up to the front gate of the grand house. Half an hour late. Lakshmi knew that she was in for a scolding. The young girl was hopeful that her future mother-in-law would not be cross with her like her own mother was. Lakshmi knew how important today was and all through the ride up, was punishing herself for this slip.
She hadn’t meant to fall asleep in the lush fields of the Gangalee flowers. It had been so peaceful today, and she had never truly had the chance to enjoy its beauty because she was always rushing to the next chore. She prayed that this one misstep wouldn’t cost her this opportunity. She was so desperate to leave
this mundane life. This small village had trapped generations of women before her, who had no choice but to continue working on the cardamom estate. It was an endless, tireless cycle. She needed to break free and this was the only way out. She prayed hard on the ride over to Goddess Lakshmi herself, pleading with her to save her from embarrassment, however the Goddess could.
The panicked girl was about to step out, but a rich voice called out to her from the driver’s seat to stop. The driver came round and opened the door for her and said, “Madam.” Lakshmi was astonished. For the first time in her life, someone had called her “Madam.” She gently smiled at him, but her joy quickly evaporated when she saw Gayatri who frantically questioned why Lakshmi was so late.
Gayatri had started scolding Lakshmi but before she could get too far into the grilling, the driver, Vinod, stepped in. “Madam, please forgive me. I had a flat tire and had to change it. This is why we are late. Apologies, Madam,” Vinod said as he bowed his head in front of Gayatri madam. Gayatri’s rage at Lakshmi melted away suddenly as she realized her anger as misplaced. In that moment, Lakshmi turned briefly to look at Vinod, shocked by his generosity towards her. She had prepared herself to beg for forgiveness from Gayatri and now was left speechless by the driver’s kindness. She gave him a quick smile to thank him.
“Okay, okay. Next time you can call me, Vinod,” she bellowed curtly at the driver. “That’s why we have given you a cell phone. I was sick with worry. She is now my daughter and my responsibility. She is just as precious to us after my Vishnu, okay? Her every move now is a reflection of my son and this family, so I need to be informed of her movement over the next month till the wedding,” she said as she dismissed Vinod.
Vinod nodded dutifully and walked back to the car. As he walked away, Lakshmi admired his silhouette not having noticed him during the car ride over. Although he was dark in complexion, he had striking features and was perhaps eight or ten years older than her. He had a strong jawline and wavy, thick black hair. She had no idea why he had showed her such kindness in that moment and thanked Goddess Lakshmi for sending her a savior in the guise of Vinod. She couldn’t wait to tell her mother when she got home and made a promise to herself that she would never be late again.
~
The training that took place over the course of the month ahead of the wedding was quite intense. Not only did it involve learning to cook Vishnu’s favorite dishes that Lakshmi had never even heard of, but also how to dress, what jewelry and make up to use, how to address businessmen and their wives and more skills that Lakshmi would never need in this small village. Gayatri was well aware of the challenges that Lakshmi would face having never been in elite social circles. She knew she had to drill in as many lessons as she could before the wedding as many of her son’s business counterparts would be coming to the wedding. She remembered how steep her own learning curve had been and she never had the luxury of the training she was providing to her future daughter-in-law. In Lakshmi’s eyes, Gayatri saw the same hunger that she once had when in Lakshmi’s position. She thought to herself, “The village mystic is right. This girl is the best choice and eager to learn. She is very charming, but I need to teach her to speak up more and know when to. She is no longer a field hand in some else’s cardamom estate, but off to become one of the most powerful wives of Kochi elite.”
~
Lakshmi was indeed a quick and astute learner. In the kitchen, she vigilantly observed Cook as she taught her how to debone fish, how to peel skin off chicken thighs, and how to marinate goat, all for the first time having been a strict vegetarian her entire life. Despite hailing from the priest class, the family had made this exception for Vishnu. They ignored that he ate the flesh of animals, which was against the tenets of their religion. They reasoned that he was allowed to, one because he was a man and two it was needed to fit in around the rich and famous in Kochi. Alas, that is what God had willed after all.
At first, Lakshmi gagged at the smell and touch of meat but she soon grew used to it but vowed to Goddess Lakshmi never to eat it herself. Early on in her trainings, she sat with her mother and Trisha one evening and told them about her day with Cook. She told them how she was made to cook meat and that she abhorred the smell left on her hands after handling the slimy flesh. She felt it was an abomination to eat nonvegetarian and she desperately wanted to tell her future mother-in-law, who she affectionately called “Maa ji” that she couldn’t and didn’t want to do it anymore. Devi was equally appalled that they were teaching her to cook meat. She nodded in agreement with her daughter, but Trisha stepped in with her unsolicited counsel.
“Devi, Lakshmi,” she said as she stared intently first at Devi, then at Lakshmi before continuing. “These rich people are different from us.” She took an intentional pause, then said, “Our gods don’t judge them in the same way they judge people of our level. Vishnu is a wealthy man who lives in Kochi. It is a big city with different customs. Gayatri madam is only trying to teach you those ways so you can adjust now and and not be shocked when you move. The big city is very different, child.
Don’t quarrel now. It will do you well to keep quiet and you will benefit in the long run. Once you are married and your life with Vishnu is stable, you can slowly change the ways of your husband.”
As Trisha explained, Devi listened, slowly nodding in agreement. She admired how her friend held such wisdom and worldly views, even though they both were only a few months apart in age and lived similar lives in this small village.
And for the next month, the training to shape Lakshmi into the model wife of a prestigious Kochi businessman continued. It involved exhausting and tiresome days. And Lakshmi was never late again after the first day’s mishap. She never wanted to experience that feeling of dread of nearly having destroyed her only chance to leave this measly village and a life with limited opportunities. If it hadn’t been for Vinod stepping in, that may very well have been it for Lakshmi. She was so grateful for his generosity and still couldn’t understand why he had been so kind towards her. As he bussed her to and from her home, she would catch Vinod’s gaze occasionally in the rearview mirror and they would exchange quick smiles. He had this twinkle in his deep brown eyes that was so endearing. When she got out of the car, on occasion, her hand would brush lightly against his and she felt a warmth in her body. Some nights she would catch herself dreaming of him, then would scold herself for thinking of another man. Yet, she looked forward each day to these small interactions with Vinod and just couldn’t understand why her heart fluttered so much in his presence. Perhaps it was the absence of Vishnu. Perhaps it was part of becoming a woman and her limited experience around men.
~
In the week leading up to the wedding, the village’s designated
event space transformed into a grand wedding venue for the largest nuptial celebrations the villagers had ever had. Everybody had been invited. Everybody but the untouchables, of course. Separate wedding favors had been arranged to be dispatched to their families ahead of the big day. No one wanted to see their disgusting beings on such a joyous occasion, even those who willed the wedding to fail.
Prior to the wedding day, Gayatri invited Devi and Trisha over to view Lakshmi’s fitting with the stylist. Several outfits she had been chosen with the stylist who she had invited from a nearby, more metropolitan-like town. In her mind, Gayatri anticipated at least ten dress changes over the four-day affair. Trisha had advised Devi not to provide any input into the process and that she should appear grateful even when she disagreed with the style choices. After all, the groom’s family was spending their money on the entire, lavish affair.
“They are so noble. They know how beautiful your daughter is and what a wonderful match she makes for their only son. I know you’ve always dreamed of choosing your daughter’s wedding dress, but we should be happy that they understand your position,” Trisha advised her friend. Despite all her encouragement, Trisha had a faint hope that the wedding would not go through, and she could still swoop in and take Lakshmi as her own daughter-in-law. The way the events were proceeding, however, her hope started to dim and she diverted her attention to fully help her best friend’s daughter, Lakshmi.
Gayatri was very nit-picky about everything that the stylist showed them. She kept harping on about how the clothes had to be perfect and of the most expensive material as many of her son’s high class business associates would be coming to the celebrations. Although she had never met any of these people or even visited her son in Kochi, his stories of over-the-top parties
and dinners made her uneasy and insecure. She didn’t want to disappoint her son in any way and so spent imprudently on every aspect of the wedding.
She had spent hours, if not days, flipping through wedding magazines she had ordered from surrounding towns to see the latest trends in fashion and demanded that the stylist show her only those, no matter what their cost. She yelled at the stylist and told him that when her one and only son would arrive for the wedding in a couple days, he should be ready with the finest silk dhotis, sherwani, shirts and a tuxedo for the reception. Devi and Trisha were silent as mice during these heated moments as Gayatri talked at the patient stylist, listing her never-ending demands. The two women were in awe of the word “taksheedo”, never having heard it before, but it sure sounded very expensive, especially since Gayatri had demanded that Vishnu was to have the finest one, specially fitted, for the reception on the final day of the celebrations.
~
Ahead of the wedding, Vishnu arrived with all pomp and circumstance. He had a grand marching band introduce his arrival. Again, he came in that classy Hero Honda and stepped out decked out in western wear. He looked taller than before in his tight dark blue jeans and Ralph Lauren polo shirt. On his wrist, he wore a watch with a large face that looked pricy and branded. He greeted his mother who dutifully stood with a large grin and her pooja plate, so smitten and proud of her son. His eyes were shaded by oversized sunglasses and his hair slicked back with grease in his usual style. After his mother’s blessings, he proceeded to greet his other family members and then, they all disappeared into their grand house as the villagers flocked
around to catch a glimpse of this star-studded, Tollywood-esque star.
~
The four days of celebrations were an exhausting affair for the groom and bridal parties and their immediate families. The whole village had been invited (except, the untouchables) and the groom and bride had to greet every single guest as the couple sat on the large stage erected in the middle of the village center and decorated with tulle and all sorts of in-season blossoms.
In particular, Gayatri was sure to include the Gangalee blossoms, not only because they showed off their local pride but because she knew these were her daughter-in-law’s favorite flowers. As each guest approached the couple, they handed them gifts, envelopes of money, and some just brought their blessings not able to afford much else.
Everyone had their cleanest and best outfits on during those days. Sparklers were handed out to the children and there was an unending spread of food and snacks, while guests enjoyed lively music and dance performances. Given the size and with no expense spared, the whole event truly felt like a momentous festival honoring the Hindu Gods. The villagers chattered, curious when Vishnu’s fellow dignitaries would attend the wedding, but none of them seemed to have showed up on any of the days.
“Strange that Vishnu’s business friends are not attending,” Vandana, the village gossip squawked.
Trisha shut down such gossip any chance she could and any rumors discrediting her best friend’s daughter and future son-in-law. She would justify their absence by replying, “Of course not. Why would they travel all the way from Kochi to this
small village on the outskirts of Idukki. They are used to certain luxuries that we cannot provide. It’s common sense.” However, Trisha couldn’t help but think the same. “Why hasn’t anyone from Kochi come to attend.”
Devi was grateful for Trisha’s friendship and strength she showed in these moments. She too had started to feel the same reservations that the villagers had voiced, but these faded away with Trisha’s reassurances.
~
The wedding day, the third of the four-day festivities, was just as action packed as the first two. The families never really had a single moment to themselves. There was always something to do or a guest to take care of. It wasn’t just the families not having any room to stop and think, but the couple didn’t have any interaction during the entire festivities. Constantly, someone was around them, whether it be some family friends or children playing around them or insisting on sitting on their laps.
In fact, the couple hadn’t ever spoken to one another. They had only spoken through their families. Lakshmi could only recall that glance she had exchanged with Vishnu, though quick, on the interview day. During their engagement, they were sat next to each other for most of that evening but didn’t talk to one another. Again, the families were always present and doing the communicating for them. She didn’t know anything about her husband, except what Gayatri, Cook or another member of Vishnu’s household had told her. His bullying and terrorizing of students and animals during the school days had long been forgotten. All was now forgiven as he had grown up and become such a powerful and well-respected city man.
As she got ready that morning for her dawn wedding, Lakshmi was nervous as it would be her first time ever with Vishnu, alone on their wedding night. Her mother and Trisha had warned her that she may not enjoy her first time with him and that over time she would learn to please her husband in ways he enjoyed most. Lakshmi wasn’t exactly sure what that meant. All she had was that momentary spark her and Vishnu had shared during the interview day and imagined that their first night would be dreamy like the passages in the Mills & Boon novels. She couldn’t wait to feel those sensations so descriptively painted in those romance novels that Kanika madam would share with her and the other girls over afternoon tea. Her fears of being with a man receded as the thought of being in the romantic embrace of her man. Excitement replaced her anxieties.
Rani was over at Lakshmi’s helping her get ready for her wedding. She felt a bit of a sting that her friend was getting married first and would escape this small village so young. Plus, she couldn’t help but feel envy as her best friend was marrying into the most prestigious family in the village and despite Lakshmi’s family being so below Vishnu and his family’s social status. Life had always been unfair for Rani after her mother had passed. Despite all the heavy feelings, Rani compartmentalized them so at least she could enjoy the pleasures of the celebrations.
Rani felt fortunate to be dressed in a delicate green crepe sari. It was the prettiest outfit she had ever worn or even owned. Gayatri had gifted it to her as Lakshmi’s future mother-in-law had done so for all of Lakshmi’s family and closest friends that were part of the bridal party. She wanted everyone at her son’s wedding and in the wedding photographs and video to look at least respectable, even if she couldn’t control their lack of class and manners that came naturally from good breeding. For Rani,
though, she saw it as an opportunity to perhaps snag one of Vishnu’s rich Kochi friends. She was disappointed when none had come, but yet marveled at how beautifully she cleaned up. A queen just as in her name.
When Devi and Trisha stepped out, the girls giggled over Lakshmi’s impending wedding night. Rani swirled around in her sari and pretended to be Lakshmi swooning at the picture frame with Vishnu’s photo. As they had seen in countless romance song numbers in the Tollywood movies, Rani pantomimed a shy bride teasing her husband with a Gangalee blossom and luring him into their bed, where they would officially become husband and wife. Lakshmi’s cheeks flushed in embarrassment at Rani’s mischief, and they both erupted into loud laughter. Devi and Trisha overhead the contentment and silently smiled at one another over the girls’ playfulness and chattered to one another about their own wedding nights.
~
In the distance, they heard the drums, the clarinets, and the trumpets blasting a festive wedding tune as the groom’s party approached the bride’s home. Trailing behind, a hand-carved ornate doli covered in Gangalee blossoms was carried by four village men. She chose not to focus on the negative interactions from earlier, in that moment. She chose to focus on all the positive blessings that Goddess Lakshmi herself was showering her with.
The make-up artist worked through the long locks of Lakshmi’s hair that Lakshmi had washed that morning. She gently undid any knots and applied a light coat of chemical hair protectant instead of the coconut oil Lakshmi’s hair had been used to all of her life. With the curling iron, Lakshmi’s hair was styled and sculpted around her oval face. Then, the sparkling tiara was anchored onto the crown of her head. As Lakshmi looked at herself in the mirror, she saw herself transform from that little skinny village girl who had picked cardamom pods most all of her life into a gorgeous, sophisticated woman. “This is how Kanika madam must feel all the time. This is just a small taste of what is to come, Lakshmi,” she said to herself as she sat there admiring herself in the mirror.
And as the make-up artist proceeded with a delicate layer of make-up, her beauty was further unlocked and she was couldn’t help but feel like one of the Femina magazine cover page models. “Vah, vah. Just wait till I finish up with the make-up. Gayatri madam, your daughter-in-law will be dynamite. There isn’t a woman in all of Kochi this stunning!” the make-up artist said genuinely.
Gayatri stood there with the utmost pride. She was so happy she was able to snag Lakshmi before she got spoiled by one of those low-level men in Lakshmi’s class. She wanted this pure flower all for her son. Gayatri smiled and replied, “Enough now, just focus
on getting her ready. Here, put some kajal to ward off the evil eye from my Vishnu’s bride.”
The make-up artist continued on, keeping the eye make-up light and playful and not heavy laden with kajal as she would typically do on other, more mainstream brides. She opted for a silver shimmering eye shadow, which uplifted Lakshmi’s eyes, letting them sparkle even brighter than ever before. For the lips, a deep rose-pink shade was applied. Lakshmi gently kissed a tissue to blot the lipstick application per the directions of the make-up artist. As in the past days, the make-up artist applied a highlighter to accentuate her naturally defined cheekbones and the curves of her face. And finally, after almost an hour and half of working Lakshmi’s hair and applying make-up, a setting spray was applied.
“Here, beta, drink this,” Gayatri said as she handed Lakshmi a glass of kesar-infused sweetened milk with a straw. “And before you get into your dress, make sure you run to the bathroom. You may not have time to eat or use the facilities. And eat some of these nuts.”
And soon evening was upon them and it was almost time for the reception. As a final step, the make-up artist and her assistant helped Lakshmi into the intricate gown that much like the wedding dress weighed at least ten kilograms. She slipped into custom-made lavender heels that matched her dress color exactly and with that, she was ready to join her husband who was now dressed in his tailored “taksheedo” for the big, final night, but now as “husband & wife.”
“Wow, he looks so very handsome in the “taksheedo”,” she thought as she smiled in disbelief to be standing next to such a sophisticated gentleman. Never in her life had Lakshmi imagined that she would transcend her social status and to do so
at such a young age. “I can forgive his small temper outbursts if it’ll mean a lifetime of luxury for me,” she reasoned with herself.
With everyone in the family dressed and ready to go, the photographer and videographer on standby began taking several shots of the multi-generational family in front of the Tulsi plant in the courtyard, then in the living room, and finally outside the mini mahal of the small village on the outskirts of the Idukki hills. A horse-drawn carriage awaited the bride and groom followed by cars to transport the rest of the family in style to the reception in the village square. The procession along with the band was ready to make the short journey to the event. Gayatri had truly spared no expense. She ensured that her family would make a spectacular, jaw-dropping entrance and she could rub it in the face of the rest of Ramdev’s family who had always treated her as a lesser person because of her upbringing.
With pride, Gayatri held her head higher than she had ever before. Her only son had a bombshell of a wife. Even though Lakshmi did not come from money, Gayatri knew that every villager was astonished by Vishnu’s bride and her natural God given beauty that Gayatri used exquisite clothing and style to accentuate and reveal. She would be the subject of gossip for months and Gayatri could feel the women in her social circle already jealous, wishing they had plucked this fresh blossom for their own bachelor sons. The crop of potential brides in their social class were bumpkins in front of Vishnu’s bride.
“Sure, they can marry some woman of similar social status, but Lakshmi’s beauty will attract attention and more wealth to my Vishnu just as the village mystic has predicted. I can already feel the positive change in his future. Honey, not vinegar, will catch more flies. Vishnu’s bride is pure honey,” she thought as she sat in her expensive peacock blue sari covered in intricate embellishments and heavy embroidery which she matched with
an elegant sapphire choker, earrings and ring set. She sat there, a very proud woman and mother.
~
Vishnu couldn’t take his eyes off his stunning partner. Everyone’s eyes were focused all on her and not on him. As they sat in the carriage, paraded like a royal couple, he whispered naughty phrases into her ear of what he wanted to do to her tonight.
Lakshmi was blushing by the things he was saying. She shrugged it off as things that married people said to each other all the time. She continued to feel so blessed and was so grateful to Goddess Lakshmi. She still could not get over her good fortune and the opportunity to escape the shackles of village life that her mother, grandmother, and generations before wore. She was going to finally escape the bars of this prison hidden in the outskirts of the hills of Idukki.
As their carriage drew closer to the event, all the villagers were gathered and were silenced by the grandeur and beauty of the couple, in particular taken aback by Lakshmi’s transformation. She was even more ethereal than she had looked over the past few days.
“Wow, the Prince Charles and Princess Diana of our small village,” shrieked a villager with joy.
“Who?” said another.
“They look like British royalty, you stupid small-minded fool. Just see Devi’s daughter’s kismet. Goddess Lakshmi has not rained, but stormed good wealth and fortune on that child and her family,” an envious distant relative spat out. “My Chandana is just as, if not more, sweet and darling. That’s why I named her after the pure white moon. Lakshmi is a shadow in my
Chandana’s presence. Only if Vishnu had seen her first, it would have been his and her good fate.”
As she sat atop the Cinderella-esque carriage, Lakshmi felt like she and Vishnu were a Tollywood power couple. With the photographer snapping pictures in fast succession, she felt like a celebrity with her own paparazzi hungry to capture the star couple’s arrival. A red carpet had been unraveled as the carriage stopped in front of the village square, and Vishnu jumped out of the carriage like the hero of a Tollywood super hit. He came around to her side, took her delicate, fair hand into his, and helped her down. As she stood on the red carpet, the glory of her dress unraveled around her as the tulle underskirt revealed the sparkling lengha she had on. She was tantalizing and she could feel the heat of all the eyes glued to her in envy and awe.
She accompanied Vishnu down the red carpet and then up to the stage, followed by Gayatri, Ramdev and the rest of the extended family. Devi, Trisha, her grandmother, and Rani were seated with the rest of the family at the front of the venue and you could see the tears of joy in each of their eyes. Lakshmi’s father was also very content, but for a very different reason. He had not only managed to have his daughter married off at the right age, but to a wealthy family and suitable groom at minimal cost to him. She was not going to be the burden he had thought she would be. He thought to himself, “Yes, the village mystic was on point. This girl is good luck for our family. And the connection has only elevated my status amongst our class. People are now calling me, what a lowly driver,
sir
. Vah! Goddess Lakshmi. Vah!”
~
No expense had been spared by Gayatri for the reception. The event was grander than the wedding itself. She was expecting all
sorts of high-ranking business dignitaries of her only and most beloved, Vishnu, to attend. Disappointingly and only moments before their procession left their home, Vishnu had informed her that due to a critical meeting with foreign partners from Dubai, they would not be able to attend. As a saving grace, however, the gracious Mr. Iyer and his family who had provided former bad boy, Vishnu, a sanctuary in the city when he needed straightening out, were among the elite Kochi friends attending. Gayatri was at least glad for that. Additionally, one of Vishnu’s junior associates, Dravid D’Souza, an Anglo-Indian from Goa who had found similar success in the big city, would be there.
“Namaskaram, Iyer ji,” Ramdev with Gayatri alongside greeted Mr. Iyer, his wife, and the odd relative. “Thank you so much for this honor. We are so very grateful for everything you have done for our son and family. Thank you for coming all this way to bless our children. We are assured you will continue to look after the two of them in Kochi, sir,” they both said as they bowed before Mr. Iyer and his wife in gratitude with their palms pressed together. They continued their conversation with Mr. Iyer and his clan as Vishnu broke away with Lakshmi to introduce her to Dravid.
“Come, come, my Lakshmi flower,” Vishnu said as he dragged her to meet Dravid. “This is Dravid from Goa. He is the party animal of the two of us. We’ll have lots of fun in Kochi in his company. He knows the best booze and parties.”
“Why, hello, beautiful lady. You are right, Vishnu brother. Aishwarya Rai pales in her comparison, Vishnu sir. Wow, what a body and those curves,” Dravid smiled as his eyes went from matching her gaze and worked its way slowly down her neck, bosoms and rested on the exposed part of her mid-riff. He then looked back up at her almond eyes and gave her a cheeky wink that Vishnu didn’t catch. “Lucky vixen, Lakshmi. I’m going to
have a hard time competing for Vishnu’s attention with you around.”
In that moment, Lakshmi forced a polite smile but underneath felt a strange discomfort in Dravid’s presence. Then and there, Rani ran up to them. “Vishnu, if you don’t mind, may I have a few moments with your bride. You are taking my best friend away tomorrow. Please. Please spare few moments of her time.” Without waiting for Vishnu’s answer, Rani whisked Lakshmi away so the girls could chat one last time before Lakshmi left for Kochi the next day. As they ran off, Lakshmi could feel Vishnu’s fiery stare as Rani pulled her away.
“Lakshmi! Lakshmi! You look even more stunning than ever before! When you go to Kochi, please don’t forget me. Your best friend. Write me as often as you can, please! Tell me everything! I cannot wait to see you next.”  Without letting Lakshmi have a word edge-wise, Rani kept rambling on, “Oh, whenever that will be. I hope that Goddess Lakshmi will bring me fortune too. Pray for me, Lakshmi that I too will soon be in Kochi and again close to you, sister.”
The two girls who had known each other from childhood looked deeply into each other’s eyes with both sadness for having to leave each other’s sides after years together. On the other end, they were also full of excitement at the prospect that at least one of them would leave this little village on the outskirts of the Idukki hills that had run the exact same way from sunrise to sundown for generations now.
~
DJ Kochi Sahib had the music blasting and the alcohol continued to flow freely as the celebrations went on. Vishnu had now been at the bar with Dravid throwing back shots and drinks
for hours, while Lakshmi was now being herded around by Gayatri through the crowds to meet her friends and bid farewell to her village folk. Lakshmi kept looking back at Vishnu worried that tonight would be another repeat of their wedding night.
Gayatri saw her concern and whispered into Lakshmi’s ears, “This is normal behavior for our men. Don’t look so shocked. When he’s back in Kochi, he has to go back to being sensible again.” And with that, Gayatri chuckled and moved on to chat with another close acquaintance.
Lakshmi wasn’t sure what to make of that comment. In her innocence, she accepted the answer but deep down in her heart, that explanation didn’t appease her but she couldn’t quite understand why. The night raged on and in the wee hours of the morning Gayatri, Lakshmi, the rest of the women folk of their family returned home with their kids in tow, leaving the men behind to wrap up the festivities.
They each retired to their respective rooms. After undressing and putting on her cotton salwar kameez for bed, Lakshmi sat up still in the bed, shuddering at every little noise, afraid it was a drunk, raging Vishnu. From the wind knocking the shutters of the windows, to the creak of doors, to the cricket who chirped in the silence of the night, she kept an eye open in case Vishnu came in and hurt her. However, Vishnu never did come back to their bed that night and eventually, she drifted off into a sweet slumber on their bed, feeling a sense of relief.
~
“Lakshmi, Lakshmi. Where are you now, girl?” she heard her Maa ji call as she suddenly woke up. “It’s almost time for you and Vishnu to head to Kochi.”
Lakshmi checked the clock at the bedside. It was six in the morning. A half hour too long that she had slept in that day. “Hahn, hahn, Maa ji. I’m just getting ready.”
She quickly got dressed, skipping a bath and wore one of the delicate plain pink silk saris gifted to her by Gayatri. She then rushed over to the kitchen to assist Cook with the breakfast duties. Today, Gayatri asked that she make her special cardamom tea as requested by Ramdev.
“We won’t see you two for a while. We all want to have a cup of that delicious tea to remember you by,” Gayatri said as she smiled and left the kitchen where Cook was preparing breakfast and Lakshmi started on her tea. Gayatri was in no mood to scold the girl for her tardiness this morning as they would be off on their trip to Kochi soon and wanted to send them off with positive blessings. Plus, doing so, would be inauspicious as they were traveling to Vishnu’s 3BHK overlooking the beautiful waters and harbor of Kochi for the first time as a couple and she didn’t want bad energy entering their marital home.
Gayatri was so proud of her son’s accomplishments. She had never ventured out of her grand home in the small village on the outskirts of the Idukki hills, but Vishnu had shown her a portfolio of pictures of his apartment, with the lush furnishings and the jaw-dropping views. She would probably never have a chance to visit, but she was pleased with herself for finding a flawless bride matching her son and his future success.
~
As Lakshmi served breakfast, she kept looking out the window to see where Vishnu had gotten to. After she had seen to it that the village elder, Ramdev, and all the males of the family had been
fed, she quietly crept away from her breakfast time duties to see if he happened to be sleeping somewhere else in the house.
As she approached Gayatri and Ramdev’s room, she heard Gayatri talking to someone in a hushed voice. She got closer to that bedroom and stood just shy of the bedroom window that looked out into the courtyard.
“Help me undress him and change him into some clean clothes. Ouf, vomit everywhere and he stinks of alcohol. Boys will always be boys and Vishnu is the prime example. And that friend of his, Dravid! Where did you find him this time?” Gayatri continued on as she spoke to another person in the room.
“Yes, Gayatri madam,” she heard Vinod’s voice. “Ah, um, the usual place. By the shanty with Dravid sir.
Lakshmi’s heart sank. She was very familiar with that place. She had heard many stories of that place and how Rani’s father would stumble home from there on late nights after drinking, followed by a visit to that local brothel. The brothel had women from the untouchable class, which the villagers conveniently looked the other way when their men frequented the place.
Rani had been there on occasion to pull her drunk father out from there and bring him back home. She described the shanty as a long, rusty tin shed with large cots everywhere separated into “rooms” with tacky, filthy curtains made from discarded rags, sewn together to create a veil of privacy. Men and women interlocked, sometimes in multiples, performing all sorts of degrading activities. Whips, swings, masks. Unmentionable items.
Rani tried to describe the scent to Lakshmi, but couldn’t quite provide a good comparison. She would gag at the thought of that smell. She would close her eyes and shake her head whenever she tried to describe that place. Rani never stayed there too
long. Only enough time to drag her good-for-nothing father out. Eventually, however, she stopped caring about her selfish father and realized that he would eventually find his way back home even if she didn’t go there to retrieve him.
“Okay. Now, Vinod, go ask Cook for the hangover cure and get some jugs of cold water to throw in my shweet boy Vishnu’s face. Just like back in the days. Oh my dear son, I’m going to miss you, my shweet boy,” Lakshmi could hear her Maa ji fawn over her disgrace of a son. “And come straight back. Don’t let anyone see you,” she hollered at Vinod as he left the room.
Vinod walked out, then past her. Lakshmi thought she had been quiet enough to go unnoticed, but he caught a glimpse of her rose-colored sari. Their eyes interlocked momentarily and, in that glance, a whole conversation was exchanged. Vinod was speechless and regretful that the young, sweet innocence of this village girl would quickly be sapped away by his boss and his despicable ways. He had learned a long time ago that it was none of his business. He had his own issues and he was unable to help her or anyone else. He liked the job and the free room and board that came with it. She wasn’t worth giving that up. Lakshmi, not yet understanding the full scope of the daunting life ahead of her, did realize the gravity of her situation in that moment, but she was also enticed by the prospect of leaving this small village and its suffocating walls behind.
~
When she walked back to the where breakfast was served, she quietly took her seat with the others at the dining table. All the men, but Vishnu had been served breakfast to their satisfaction, and the women had now sat down to start their meals.
“Lakshmi dear, where is your handsome partner?” the village elder addressed Lakshmi directly.
The bubble of dull pain she felt as she sat there burst as she heard those words. She straightened up, smiled and let out a sweet giggle without offering much more. She played the demure new bride, even though her heart sank fast, deep in her chest. “Remember, Goddess Lakshmi is with you. This is the golden ticket She has bestowed on you. Once in Kochi, everything will be okay,” she thought to herself as she pecked on the flattened rice in her aval upma and sipped the special cardamom tea that she had made earlier. Cook had kept the tea hot for everyone to enjoy.
After a while, Gayatri swiftly came in to be seated next to Ramdev and smiled at Lakshmi. She proceeded to have her breakfast and cup of tea. Everyone was engrossed in either the newspaper, feeding their kids, or just deep in their thoughts.
“Is your son not going to join us, again?” the old man pointedly asked Ramdev and Gayatri. “Too much, last night?” He pantomimed drinking. Everyone but Gayatri and Lakshmi let out a roar of laughter. Gayatri looked at Lakshmi and let out a light-hearted smile and said, “Oh, boys will be boys, Papa ji,” she replied, feigning pride to conceal her embarrassment. It seemed to be such a normal occurrence in the household that no one was very concerned about Vishnu’s night of debauchery. The newlyweds were set to leave later that morning to their new lives in Kochi, so the family and their servants moved on and turned their focus to hasten those preparations.
With everything packed and ready to go, it wasn’t till a bit after 2pm that afternoon that a tired and heavily hungover Vishnu appeared, now all cleaned up but still dazed. His eyes were sunken and his breath still lingered of alcohol and vomit, despite attempts to brush the smell of his drunken night away.
After putting some food away, the couple was ready to head out on their journey. Gayatri and Ramdev were at the forefront of the farewell party. Gayatri had her trusty puja plate ready and lit. Vishnu came and placed his hand on the small of Lakshmi’s back and gave her one of his charming smiles, reigniting hope in Lakshmi. “Boys will be boys,” she thought as her anxiety calmed. “I’m a grown woman now and this is how men just act. Once in Kochi, everything will be fine.”
Lakshmi was too young and had very little guidance from the women around her to understand a man like Vishnu and that they just didn’t change. Her parents and family had handed her off to her new family and it was for her to forge her own path forward. They had done all they could.
Despite the unease she felt around him, Lakshmi had no choice but to move forward. If she did go home now or ever, it would only bring disgrace and shame to the family. She would be spoiled goods. “This is your chance, Lakshmi. This is your chance to leave this small village life behind. Think about taht,” she repeated to herself. With that, Lakshmi entered the car in the front passenger seat of Vishnu’s Hero Honda. They were ready to embark on a completely fresh journey as man and wife. Lakshmi would have no family and no friends in Kochi. It would truly be a new beginning for her with a man she hardly knew.
~
Chapter 4 – Envy or Maatsarya
“The spirit of envy can destroy; it can never build.”
~ Margaret Thatcher
Lakshmi did a small prayer to her namesake Goddess as she sat seated in the Hero Honda in the passenger seat next to her
husband. Her status had been elevated from the poor village girl from the labor class that lived in small two-room house with her father, mother, grandmother, brother, cousin-brother and occasional relative to “madam”. She would no longer have to wake up in the wee hours to fetch water from the well and cook rice before she labored on the cardamom estate wondering what life as Kanika madam was like.
As she sat next to her husband, who was recovering from last night’s hangover, she no longer thought about the terrifying wedding night she had or that her drunk husband had pleasured himself at a brothel the night before. She had now said goodbye to her family and now to his as well. They had no immediate plans to come back to the village and it was rare for Vishnu to have come home, so she had to focus on her future. Her future in Kochi.
She caught herself looking at herself in the side view mirror and she couldn’t help but smile. She was leaving this hamster wheel of a life. The first woman out of many generations.
It had been about ten minutes into the ride when Vishnu suddenly veered off of the dusty main road onto the unofficial road towards a dingy shanty, breaking Lakshmi’s contemplation. This road had been smoothed into place over the years as various vehicles made their way and parked in this unmarked district on the outer edge of the village. Vishnu rolled down the windows and Lakshmi inhaled that unfamiliar stench of degradation, debauchery and deception that Rani had only tried to describe to her. She sat there looking in the side view mirror, now her face full of despair and shame. She stayed silent and did not dare to look elsewhere in case she locked eyes with a degenerate.
“Dravid, friend,” Vishnu was laughing. “Come, brother, let’s go. Fun’s over.” She now saw her husband pull a drunk fellow with
a half-buttoned shirt and his fly half open stumble towards the car.
“Ha Ha! What a night, brother. These village girls know how to rock. Not like the spoilt city bitches. Cheap too,” Dravid responded in his stupor.
Lakshmi held it together but felt so much anguish and incomprehensibility of the situation. “Dravid is not a good man or influence on Vishnu. This will have to change once we are in Kochi,” she thought to herself. Once in Kochi she knew things would be different and she closed her eyes and thought of her grandma who would often say, “Do not be discouraged by slow progress. A snail can climb a mountain if it keeps going."
Dravid threw himself into the backseat. He laughed stupidly as he gazed aimlessly at the car’s ceiling. Then he caught Lakshmi’s stern stare, smiled at her, and said, “Hello beautiful.” Lakshmi felt cold disgust from the way he spoke to her and she quickly looked away.
Vishnu jumped into the driver’s seat and yelled loudly, “Kochi, here we come!” He moved across the gear shift to Lakshmi and gave her a peck on her cheek. “Let’s go, baby!” he exclaimed in English. Lakshmi blushed and looked away feeling a bit embarrassed from his public affection and also amazed by his command of the foreign language. “He is so classy and worldly,” she admired. He laughed a hearty laugh, put the key in the ignition, and off the trio went towards Kochi. Five minutes into the ride a loud snoring Dravid could be heard in the backseat, but Lakshmi made sure that Dravid didn’t disturb the warm and loving connection she was feeling with Vishnu in that moment.
~
The car ride wasn’t as long as Lakshmi had thought it to be.
It was only about four hours away with traffic from their small village on the outskirts of the hills of Idukki. Nevertheless, Lakshmi was very excited. She had seen photos of their 3BHK overlooking the Arabian Sea on Marine Drive. Over the course of the drive, she fell into a daydream as her hero of a husband drove his Hero Honda towards her new home. Remembering the pictures, she recalled that the entry way itself was so huge and housed a large statute of the God Vishnu and his wife, Goddess Lakshmi, beckoning them and their guests into their lavish marble floored mahal.
The living room opened out onto a large balcony that overlooked the beautiful backwaters and she could just imagine what the salty sea air would feel like when it touched her skin. The kitchen was straight out of a Tollywood movie. Fine finishes and hot and cold water that flowed directly from the tap. And not to mention the two sprawling bathrooms fitted with tubs so she could take a shower or a bath whenever she pleased. There were so many bedrooms. One for her and Vishnu. One for their guests. And surely, one for their future son. Lakshmi was so very happy as she thought about her beautiful home that they inched towards and the endless opportunities to come.
If Rani ever visited or whenever they met next, she would tell her all about how luxurious life was now as Lakshmi madam. “Oh Rani, you won’t believe it! I don’t even have to move a muscle. I worry I’m going to put on weight just eating all the delicious foods and our maid taking care of all the household chores,” she would brag to her best friend just as Kanika madam would. “I hope you too can find a man that can provide you with all these comforts. No, you definitely will my dear friend. I’ll make sure of it and connect you with one of Vishnu’s business partners.” She was pleased with herself as Lakshmi thought about how she
would tell her best friend about her new life in Kochi and find a way for Rani to join her in the big city.
As they drove further out from the village, she marveled at the winding roads and the changing beauty that she was seeing for the very first time in her life. So many firsts this week, Lakshmi thought. Riding in a luxury car, beautiful clothes, servants and drivers. She was living a life she had only read in the many books her grandmother would smuggle to her or heard of from the many stories that Kanika madam would tell during their weekly afternoon teas. They drove on long stretches of highways and halfway through the trip, they stopped at a rest stop for lunch. They sat at a table and instead of it being a romantic first lunch as a married couple, Dravid plonked down next to Lakshmi.
“Brother, bring us two teas and a cold ThumsUp for him,” Vishnu ushered to a young boy taking the orders and pointed at Dravid when gesturing who the ThumsUp was for.
Vishnu and Dravid looked over the menu posted on the road stop shack as Lakshmi meekly sat at one of the plastic chairs at the table for four. “Come here again, brother. One goat biryani and one veg one. Quickly, we are in a hurry,” Vishnu yelled at the boy. The young boy nodded, acknowledging the order and then rushed to the make-shift roadside kitchen.
The two men chatted and then disappeared behind the shack into the fields to relieve themselves. Lakshmi sat there quietly, feeling a bit uncomfortable as she was alone for the first time in a strange place where she knew literally no one. The tea and ThumsUp came and she sipped on her tea as she waited for her husband and his good-for-nothing friend to return.
Lakshmi was excited to eat out with a delicious meal at a proper restaurant for the first time. Thinking back, she didn’t remember ever eating outside her home except perhaps at a
friend’s house or at the festivals in their small village. She thought, “Wow, how yummy this tea is and to have it in this beautiful open-air restaurant. This is what wealth must feel like.” She sat there taking in her surroundings, feeling like a princess.
The men returned to the table and Vishnu ushered Lakshmi to go as well as the journey ahead was another two hours or so. It took Lakshmi a moment to understand what he was implying and she then rushed off to find a place behind the shack where other women huddled to relieve themselves. She went to the well and quickly washed her hands before returning to the table as she felt a little scared being out on her own in unfamiliar surroundings.
Vishnu and Dravid were already knee deep into their biryani. “Vah, top biryani, my friend. Best hangover food ever,” said a now sobered up Dravid. He pointed to Lakshmi, “Eat, eat. Don’t be shy. Veg one for you, but now you’re not a virgin, try the goat,” Dravid roared into laughter as did Vishnu at his sordid joke.
Lakshmi awkwardly smiled as the two shoveled food into their mouths as Lakshmi delicately ate a few bites. She wasn’t accustomed to eating first along with the men. This too felt out of her comfort zone, but she recognized that she would have to adapt to the new rules. She couldn’t act like a silly village girl.
Once done, Dravid let out a loud and unseemly belch. “Vishnu, what an idea you had to stop here. You made my day. Let’s get something sweet at the mithai store and get those delicious Ras Malai,” Dravid suggested to his friend.
Vishnu laughed and in reply said, “You have such a sweet tooth, you devil.”
“Not all of us can peck on something sweet now whenever we want,” Dravid slyly responded as he grinned at Lakshmi. He covertly placed his hand on Lakshmi’s upper thigh, rubbing it gently as he joked. Lakshmi didn’t move and stared straight ahead at her husband. Luckily, Vishnu hadn’t noticed, otherwise drama would ensue and she just couldn’t endure another painful encounter that day. “Dravid is no good for us,” Lakshmi quietly noted to herself, “Snake of a man.
