As they were walking back to the apartment, they talked more about themselves to one another. Lakshmi told Ruby of the Gangalee fields back home and how she used to sit in the mornings under the sun and sketch. “You’ll have to show me your drawings some time,” Ruby told her.
Upon seeing Vishnu’s car downstairs, Ruby held Lakhsmi back and whispered to her, “The rest of the money that Judge Rao gave you, keep it somewhere hidden from everyone, even from Vishnu. From what you earn at the hotel over your wages, don’t give everything to him. He will drink it away. Keep some aside for yourself. Maybe one day you will need it. Please listen to me, Lakhsmi. You are like my little sister and I am so happy that you are here now.”
The women headed up to their floor and found Vishnu and Dravid crapped out on the couch in Vishnu and Lakhsmi’s apartment, working their way through cheap beers with a number of empty cans laying around the floor already. “What took you both so long?” an angered Vishnu asked. “Mr. Iyer let
us go early because he said my bride would be waiting and I should not keep her. Bitch! Where were you?” he questioned as he came at Lakhsmi. Ruby, once again, intervened with her body and disarmed an irate, drunken Vishnu.
“Chill, bro. She was with me. After our shift, we went shopping for household groceries. Remember, you have nothing in your fridge because you’ve been living like a dirty scoundrel all these years,” she said teasingly, but firmly. Ruby could hold her own with men like him. Vishnu was a light weight in comparison to the drunken men she had handled all her life. And her lifelong dream to break into the Tollywood royalty gave her the pleasure of meeting even more assholes like him.
“Okay, okay.,” he said, backing down. “All this with the money I gave you this morning?” His sharp stare pierced Lakhsmi who was tempted to tell him about her interaction with Judge Rao, but she heeded Ruby’s advice and averted her eyes. She knew Vishnu was a very jealous man that if she said anything like that, he would hurt her again or send her back to their village.
“Ruby, she is good at haggling. Without her, I’m not sure I would have been able to get all these things,” Lakhsmi replied looking at Ruby with a gracious smile. In that moment, Ruby was very proud of Lakhsmi for holding her own, and she also knew that Vishnu had already shown the poor girl his true colors, which didn’t surprise her at all. “He isn’t even the worst of them, which says a lot in itself,” she thought as she rolled her eyes in her head, “But this girl is hungry for opportunity like I was. She is going to be okay and tough it out with this bozo until she can leave. Maybe he’ll leave just a few bruises here and there, but she will be okay.”
With the atmosphere calmer now, the two couples sat and chatted for a while over Lakshmi’s freshly brewed cardamom tea and Parle-G biscuits. Then, the two women broke from the boys
as they watched the news and the women proceeded to put away the grocery items and prepare a simple dinner of sambar and dosas.
The late evening grew to night, and then the two couples retired to bed.
“See you tomorrow,” Ruby and Dravid said. Ruby then added, “I’ll be half an hour early tomorrow.”
“Good night,” Lakshmi said cheerfully to Ruby. She was so relieved to find a friend in her. “Thank you, Goddess Lakshmi for bringing me guidance in the form of this earth angel. Even though this is not the life I had anticipated, I know you are there guiding me and will send me in the right direction so that one day I will be living on Marine Drive, in the comfort of my home with helpers as had been promised to me,” she thought quietly to herself.
With Ruby and Dravid gone, it was just Vishnu and herself and Lakshmi dreaded every second of it. He asked her to make another cardamom tea as he sat texting on his cell phone and watching sports news. When in the kitchen, Lakshmi heard him take a call.
“Yes, yes, Mr. Iyer sir. Yes, I’ll be at the airport. 5am. Yes,” she could hear Vishnu’s deference to Mr. Iyer she had never witnessed him display in the village. She thought, “In the village, he acts like an entitled king. Here, he is Mr. Iyer’s puppy dog.” She caught herself, realizing that she was talking about her husband in an unkind way.
“Lakshmi, forget the tea. I’m heading to bed. I have to pick up a VIP in the morning, so wake me up at 3 am. Go with Ruby and Dravid tomorrow to the hotel for your shift.” And with that, he disappeared into the bedroom.
Lakshmi was relieved as she saw her husband go to bed. She had made the tea, so she was delighted that she could sit for a bit and enjoy it by herself. As she sat there, she marveled at all the fun, new experiences she had that day at the market with Ruby. Being at the hotel, however, was another matter, but her youthful exuberance allowed her to push that experience aside. Then, her phone buzzed. It was a text reply from her mother.
“Good you reach. Family is miss you, our little gem. Rgds to Vishnu ji. Good night with love.” The text brought a feeling of warmth and tears rolled down her cheeks as she thought of all the lies her parents were told about the promised life in the big city. Thankfully, her family only had one cell phone amongst themselves and limited access. Her father must have topped up the sim card today because she hadn’t heard from them in a few days.
“What would I tell them. What if they ever want to do a video call,” she thought to herself. “The shame.” And that empty feeling of despair that she had felt before returned as she sat quietly on the sofa. Eventually, exhaustion got the better of her and she drifted to sleep on the sofa as she had done the night before.
~
All of a sudden, she heard a loud ringing. Her alarm was going off. She looked at the clock on her phone. 2:35am. It had felt like only a few moments of rest that she had gotten. She was used to waking up early, having done it all her life, but today she so desperately wanted to stay sleeping. She was able to sacrifice her desire to sleep in to avoid Vishnu’s impending wrath. Plus, she thought, once he was gone, she could spend the rest of the morning cleaning the house and planting her Gangalee seeds in the pot that Basu Bhai so kindly gifted her.
She put on some water for the tea and took out the batter to make idli. It was so easy to cook with a gas stove instead of having to start the fire as she had needed to do early in the morning at her village home. She appreciated these small luxuries that she was afforded by her new life in Kochi, although not quite at the level she had been told she would have. She reset her mind, focusing on the positive highlights of her new life so as not to be bogged down with all the unpleasant realities she had been facing. She recalled her grandmother saying when Lakshmi’s mother would scold her for daydreaming, “My granddaughter, a dream doesn't become reality through magic or prayers. You will have to put in sweat, determination and hard work into it. Dare to dream, but then don’t sit around for it to come to you. Go grab it for yourself.”
As she was getting things ready in the morning, she realized she had forgotten to wake Vishnu up and it was now ten past the hour. She hurried to the bedroom and tapped him in hopes he would wake up. When he didn’t, she went and turned on the lights. He was out like a log. She then shook him gently and heard him angrily moan, then turn over. She shook him a little harder.
A disgruntled Vishnu woke. “Whaaaat?” he bellowed.
“You, uh, told me to wake you. Airport. Mr. Iyer,” she muttered.
“Okay, okay,” he said as he signaled her out of the room. As she turned to leave, he had looked at his phone. He grabbed her before she could walk safely away and pulled her towards him. “Whore. You stupid, bitch. What, did you sleep in? It’s quarter past. I told you three. Three on the dot.” With the back of his hand, he smacked her hard and his wedding ring hit her jaw bone sending a shocking pain through her delicate face. She yelped as she crouched away to protect herself in case he hit her again. “Hurry with my tea, village urchin.”
Lakshmi dared not cry in front of him and quickly did as she was told, scurrying out of the room like a scared cat. She got his tea ready. The idlis were just about ready and she heated up some sambar. She put these out on the coffee table as they didn’t have a dining area as Ruby and Dravid had at their apartment. She waited for the tyrant to come for breakfast after he freshened up in the bathroom and put on his driver’s uniform: a white shirt, black slacks and a driver’s hat.
He looked at his shoes and threw them at her, “Why haven’t you polished these yet?”
She had never polished shoes before. There was never a need to. She had no idea what to do. She grabbed one of her rags from the day before and knelt by him as he ate his breakfast and she polished his shoes the best she knew how to. “Hurry. I’m late. Crazy cunt. Put them on now,” he bellowed. She quietly obeyed and dared not meet his eyes in fear he would get even angrier at her. He’d be gone in just a short while and then she’d be free to start her day the way she wanted to. She kept this thought with herself.
She was so excited to plant the Gangalee seeds and start her little garden on the windowsill of the kitchen. She would put her Goddess Lakshmi statuette that her grandmother had given her when she was a little girl right there. Plus, she couldn’t wait for the beautiful, luminous flowers to emerge. They needed very little care to bloom as vibrantly as they did and brought her so much joy. “It would certainly brighten up this dingy place,” Lakshmi thought to herself as she put on the oaf’s shoes.
With a humph, Vishnu got up and hurriedly raced out of the apartment to his car. He was to pick up another one of Mr. Iyer’s VIP clients who was flying in from the Middle East. When he left, Lakshmi felt so relieved and took a big sigh of relief. Even
though the apartment was in tatters, his absence uplifted the entire place. In her mind, that was a small win in itself.
The very first thing Lakshmi did was to pull out her statuette, put it on the kitchen’s window sill and prayed to Goddess Lakhsmi for guidance and strength. Then, she poured the soil Basu Bhai had gifted her in the pot and bored three 2-inch holes with her index finger, spaced about an inch apart. She put two seeds in each hole and covered this with fresh soil. Then, she watered the soil.
“In a few days, we’ll start to see some sprouts and, in a few months, they will bloom. These flowers bloom all year round. Hmmmm, purple blossoms. Purple and light blue would be such a nice change to this desolate place,” she thought to herself as she busied herself with cleaning the remaining buckets and washing the floors. As she did this, she also prepped dinner and also made Rava Kesari Bhath, a simple sweet dish. She still had no idea what her and Vishnu’s schedule was going to be like, so wanted to be prepared either way.
“Daughter,” she recalled her mother’s voice, “You must be patient. In the beginning, it takes time for everything to fall in place. Don’t expect things to just happen. I’ve been with your father for now how much years. When I met him, he was just a boy. Now he is a man. But it takes a strong woman to make him that way.” Lakshmi reflected on this advice. “It’ll just take time. I must be strong. He is still just a boy.” She justified his ill treatment of her with this notion.
Lakshmi’s hands were raw from scrubbing and cleaning, but she had made great progress and was proud of how the apartment was transforming. She was able to clean the floors of all the rooms so they were no longer grimy. Actually, having washed the floors, she slowly felt a warmth to the apartment. She stood up after spending most of the morning on her knees or at the stove
and admired her work. There was no longer a lingering odor of what resembled rotting garbage and sweat. Satisfied, she took a bit of the sweet dish she had prepared earlier and fed it to the Goddess. She closed her eyes and prayed some more for more goodness to come into her life.
As she looked around at what to tackle next, she thought, “There isn’t much I can do with that sofa to get that clean. If I wash it, it will be wet and if Vishnu sits on it, the dampness will upset him. Maybe I can ask Ruby what I can do when she comes.” She then looked at the clock. “Oh, my. I must get ready.”
She found such peacefulness in the apartment with Vishnu gone. Now that it was clean, she took a much-needed bath and washed her hair, which flowed down her lower back. The water was no longer hot, but she didn’t mind that at all. She had worked up quite a sweat having done all that work and the cool water actually felt good.
She rustled through her bags and found a bottle of lotion and the perfumed oil her mother had prepared. Today, she massaged the lotion all over her body and dabbed a bit of the oil on the pulse points and a little in her locks, which she let air dry as she waited for Ruby. She felt very happy today having cleaned the house and planted the seeds for her purple blossoms. She had on a simple light blue salwar kameez, with little purple flowers and a matching blue bindi. She also decided to put on some light blush, lilac eyeshadow and kajal to accentuate her almond-shaped eyes.
She looked at herself in the mirror and admired her beauty. “Goddess Lakshmi. It is by no mistake that you have led me here to Kochi. The mystic was right. I am destined to be great and one day I will have a 3 BHK on Marine Drive.” Her concentration was broken by a loud knock on the door. It was Ruby and Dravid.
“Good morning,” Ruby greeted Lakshmi, who was so very happy to see her friend. “Wow, the apartment looks amazing and it smells so fresh.” Ruby complimented Lakshmi as she embraced her friend.
“Ruby, big sister, I made Rava Kesari Bhath. For sweet new beginnings,” Lakshmi said as she handed Ruby a bowl. She acknowledged Dravid’s existence, but didn’t really acknowledge him. She did not like him very much and that was obvious through her body language. However, Dravid seemed to be the more harmless of the two men.
“This is so delicious,” Ruby said. “Dravid, eat some. Here. You have magic in your hands, little sister.” Dravid nodded as he enjoyed the sweet dish as well, but noticing the time, urged the girls to hurry up so they weren’t late for their duty at the Kochi Queen Hotel.
~
The trio set off from their apartment building and walked about ten minutes to a busier main road where there were throngs of people waiting for a bus. Lakshmi and Ruby had taken the bus home yesterday evening and it had been busy, but today, it seemed like triple the number of people were now waiting in line. Ruby whispered to Lakshmi, “You have to fight your way in. Here, hold my hand and push your way through. Don’t apologize to anyone. Just keep going.”
Lakshmi did just that and when a large rusty container on six wheels came rattling past, she pushed her way through the crowds holding tightly onto Ruby’s hand. The bus was packed with all sorts of people. Old people that weren’t given a seat, sweaty men pressed against the next person and more closely if a woman, screaming babies, college aged students, and more.
The saving grace was that the trio had made it onto the bus and as the bus drove on, a gentle breeze swooshed into the bus compartment holding the human sardines, providing a bit of relief in the heat and sweat.
Lakshmi enjoyed this breeze. It smelled like the sea, which she couldn’t wait to go see. Ruby had mentioned that over the weekend on their day off she would convince Vishnu and Dravid that they should all have a beach day. Lakshmi had never seen the ocean before and once she did, she would be the farthest she had been from her small village on the outskirts of Idukki. “I’ve come this far,” she thought to herself as she breathed in the beautiful sea air, “Rani would absolutely love this.” As the bus rode on, Ruby noticed Lakshmi’s wistful look and remembered how the new experiences of living in the big city had once seemed so invigorating and exciting to her as well. Unfortunately, that sensation had faded over time as life became mundane and rote.
~
Day two at the hotel was much like the prior day, but less daunting to Lakshmi as a bit of the novelty had worn off. Ruby and Lakshmi swiftly changed into their uniforms that were laundered once a week, then headed to get instructions from Maria ma’am, who was curt and firm as always. She warned the girls that VIP suite 204 had an especially loud and rambunctious party in their room the night before and that the two should be extra thorough in their cleaning.
As Ruby and Lakshmi headed upstairs to start their day, hoping not to have a chance encounter with a rowdy, callous guest, Lakshmi sheepishly asked Ruby if they were going to have lunch in the kitchen again today. Lakhsmi had felt unusually excited to come to this god-awful establishment, and hadn’t yet realized
that Chef Vijaya had lit that small spark in her. He was such a gentleman and he was so talented in creating delicious flavors in the biryani she had yesterday. She felt his talents were surely wasted in a place like the Kochi Queen Hotel.
“Is it his food you like, Lakshmi?” Ruby poked fun at her friend. “Don’t forget you are a married woman. He’s off limits to you.”
Lakshmi smiled coyly, “No, big sister. I love to cook and he has so much experience in making such interesting dishes. I have never had a biryani like his before and want to know how he does it.”
“Sure, sure,” Ruby said, knowing that Chef Vijaya had broken the hearts of many of the women at the hotel. He was a bit of a player with women, but was also laser focused on his goals. Ruby knew that Chef Vijaya had loftier dreams and he wasn’t going to allow some village girl or housekeeper stop him from getting to a luxury hotel somewhere foreign like in the Middle East. He had worked so hard to network with the VIP guests, attending to their every desire. Sure, he flirted with many of the young women, but his true passion was working the small circuit of Middle Eastern sheikhs that came through the seedy establishment. He had hopes that just one would sponsor his visa to work in Dubai or Doha or somewhere fancy. Like Ruby and many others who had come to Kochi, he was working at his dream, one day at a time. And a young woman like Ruby or Lakshmi was only an obstacle to the goal.
Chef Vijaya had come to Kochi as a young man to study engineering after securing a seat with a full scholarship at the top university. This was an incredible feat for someone from a lower caste. He had achieved this and not through the misguided poverty quota system. Although extremely gifted in mathematics and design, he found the program stuffy and too competitive. Just a year and half into the program, he dropped out.
His parents were furious with him for ruining his and his family’s future. His parents disowned him, afraid that his failure would adversely affect his sister’s chances of marrying into a good home. So, Chef Vijaya remained in Kochi to shield his family from the shame.
At first, he slept on friends’ couches, but as soon as those invitations ran out, he spent some time on the streets until he met Mr. Iyer who was looking for a dishwasher/waiter and was providing room and board. Chef Vijaya leapt at the opportunity. As a dishwasher, he often found himself assisting Chef Sanjeev and over time, with his guidance guidance, Chef Vijaya found his passion for cuisine. Many years later, Chef Vijaya had worked his way up the chain to his current position and although the work was stressful, he enjoyed experimenting with different foods and spices. It seemed the guests appreciated it and he equally loved the compliments he would receive. There had been opportunities in the past to move up north to work in a three-star hotel and even offers to work at a resort in Goa, but Chef Vijaya found it hard to break his loyalty to Mr. Iyer. If Chef Vijaya was going to leave, he was going to go somewhere foreign.
~
VIP Suite 204 was exactly as Maria Ma’am had described. An absolute chaotic mess and the smell that emanated out to the hallway was unbearable. As they opened the door, they saw empty alcohol bottles lay strewn across the floor and in places smashed. There were random pools of vomit. The bedsheets were stained with blood and semen and used condoms lay everywhere. The girls sighed and looked at each other in dismay. The only saving grace was that the guests had checked out. However, they hadn’t even bother to leave a tip to cover the damages.
“Mr. Iyer will see to them,” Ruby muttered as she drew the curtains and opened the windows to allow some fresh air in.
The room didn’t just need a refresh. It needed an absolute overhaul. It was going to be a long, tiring day. With double the gloves on, the girls first tackled all the debris and took it out to the garbage chute. Next, they stripped the bed of all its sheets. The mattresses were covered with waterproof covers that seemed to have never been replaced and simply wiped down. Ruby had Lakhsmi scrub that waterproof cover as she tackled the pools of vomit. Two hours in, the girls had made significant progress. Maria ma’am came around to check, “Good. You girls are an excellent team. Mrs. Iyer is going to like you, little one. You are a hard worker and very pretty.”
Lakshmi couldn’t quite understand what her beauty had to do with Mrs. Iyer liking her or how well she was doing her job. She asked Ruby what that could possibly mean, but Ruby dismissed it as something not to worry about yet. They spent another hour getting the room to 90% of its former self before moving on to the rest of the rooms. Luckily, two of the other rooms had been unoccupied the night before and they could finish their shift with some time left to head downstairs to have lunch at Chef Vijaya’s kitchen.
~
Today, Chef Vijaya was serving up lunch thalis, which consisted of a variety of different South Indian dishes. He dished out some sambar and rice for the girls with papadum and coconut chutney. Just as yesterday’s meal had been, Lakshmi found the food so heavenly. She couldn’t help herself and she grabbed Chef Vijaya’s attention to ask what techniques and spices he used just as he was headed out for his afternoon break between shifts.
“I’ve never had sambar like this. Tell me how you make it like this?” Lakshmi pleaded.
“Oh, let Chef Vijaya go rest, Lakshmi,” Ruby said pulling Lakshmi away. “We’ll give him this trouble on his day off on Friday. The restaurant is closed that day.” Ruby shooed Chef Vijaya who would have more than willingly stayed behind to chat with Lakshmi. He found her captivating and her naivety so refreshing.
“Yes, let’s chat Friday. I’ll tell you all my trade secrets,” Chef Vijaya said with a wink directed at Lakshmi.
Lakshmi’s felt a flutter in her stomach and she responded with a warm smile. “Careful,” Ruby said, noticing the exchange. “Don’t forget you are a married woman and Chef Vijaya is a player just like all men. Don’t be fooled by his charm.”
Lakshmi and Ruby giggled at that which somewhat dampened the seriousness of Ruby’s advice. She had been burned more than once by men like Chef Vijaya and was confident he was no different. She felt very protective over Lakhsmi. Lakhsmi, on the other hand, was oblivious to the advice she received and dismissed it. She enjoyed the attention directed at her from this gentleman. It was a refreshing change to be treated with dignity, something she was certain she would never receive at home.
~
After their shift, the girls decided to walk home. It would take them an hour, but it was good to stretch out their legs before they went home. Lakhsmi told Ruby that she had made dinner enough for all of them if her and Dravid wanted to come over that evening. Ruby was more than delighted, although she wasn’t sure if Vishnu and Dravid would be home that evening.
“It all depends on the VIPs, but usually the boys will not be home till late. They may have to chauffeur some guests from different venues. You get used to the uncertainty, but at least we have each other now. It was so lonely before,” Ruby told Lakshmi with a smile. “Change of plans. How about we go to the promenade on Marine Drive today?”
Lakshmi thought that was a wonderful idea. She was still getting used to the idea that her husband was a driver and not the successful businessman that his family paraded him to be. He had duped them as well. His identity had been well hidden all these years, something that could only be possible in Kochi. She thought that if he could conceal this from a whole village, she could certainly do her part not to expose her circumstances to her family. She could not imagine the sheer pain it would bring to her family, especially to her mother and father. They had been so proud of her being married off and with no dowry. They thought of Lakshmi as their lucky charm after they endured so much loss and she would be sure to protect their honor and feelings for all the sacrifices they had made.
~
The girls walked all the way to Marine Drive, which didn’t seem as far as they had been chatting away the whole time. Lakshmi was so excited to see the sea for the first time and was glad that she didn’t have to wait for the weekend to do so. She couldn’t believe her eyes and rushed over to the promenade’s edge to reach down and touch the water.
“Careful,” Ruby said as she held Lakshmi tightly so she wouldn’t fall in. “You can’t swim. You don’t want to fall in there.”
Lakshmi splashed the water, then cupped some in her palms and threw some at Ruby. She laughed so whole-heartedly, like a little
child oblivious to the world’s woes. Ruby could feel the renewed energy of Lakshmi’s. These feelings were the same blissful joys Ruby had felt when she had left the landlocked farming village in Punjab and came down to pursue her dreams. It was so invigorating to see Lakshmi enjoy these simple moments of happiness and be momentarily blind to the garbage heaps or makeshift shelters that were scattered along the promenade.
“I’ll have to take you to Fort Kochi one day. It’s farther north from here. They have a beach area and you can feel the sand beneath your feet. Maybe this weekend we can go with the boys,” Ruby said as Lakshmi skipped on along the promenade.
The girls came to a pier area where there were a number of boats. “Ruby, Ruby. Can we go for a ride? I’ve never been on a boat, let alone on the sea,” Lakshmi excitedly pleaded with Ruby.
“No. Not today. We should head back soon. It is getting late and it’ll soon be dark. When we come with the boys over the weekend, we can take the water taxi to Fort Kochi. We’ll make a day of it,” Ruby said as she and Lakshmi headed back to their apartment building.
~
Once home, Lakshmi asked Ruby what she could do with the sofa that sat looking so defeated and depressing in the corner of the small apartment. Lakshmi didn’t have enough money to buy another one and she certainly didn’t know if scrubbing it would do it any good.
“The sofa cover comes off. Here, see. You can wash this.” Ruby said. Lakshmi was impressed and also wondered how long it had sat there covering the sofa, never having been dusted or cleaned.
“You can make your own cover and cushions to decorate and liven up the place,” Ruby continued. “I can help. I use anything
and everything I can find for my home. You can’t rely on men for taste. And you’re certainly not going to need all those clothes from your wedding. You will have no place to wear them here,” Ruby said.
Lakshmi was mortified. She couldn’t cut up these expensive, boutique clothes to decorate this apartment, but on the other hand, she realized the practicality of the situation. She could save a few pieces as a memory, but she certainly wasn’t going to any fancy business receptions or cocktail parties as she had been promised and trained for by Maa ji. She wouldn’t be hobnobbing with local dignitaries and movie stars. At least not now. For now, she was the wife of a driver and it was time for her to act like one. It would be difficult to choose which sari to use first, but she could think of that tomorrow. For now, she made her special cardamom tea for herself and her new city friend. They ripped off the stinky sofa cover to find a decently cream sofa with some staining beneath, laid one of Lakshmi’s plainer saris over it and sat down to enjoy the tea and Parle-G biscuits. Lakshmi felt like the lady of her house and pretended she was hosting a small tea party, much like the ones she would have at Kanika madam’s on her family’s cardamom estate.
~
Ruby left around ten that evening. Lakshmi was exhausted after the very long day, but the excitement of the day and seeing the Arabian Sea for the first time kept her from feeling restful. She was also anxious that Vishnu hadn’t returned. In fact, he never came home that night. She felt a bit terrified, all alone in this apartment in a new city, that was still not quite her home. She felt a bit of relief knowing that Ruby was just down the hall from her and she at least knew Ruby was someone she could trust.
She faded away into a sweet slumber sometime around four in the morning. She heard a loud knock on the door. She woke up startled. It was Ruby who had come to pick her up for the shift. A very tired Lakshmi opened her eyes that had been crusted shut from her short nap.
“Oh my god,” a flustered Lakshmi exclaimed as she opened the door to Ruby, “I slept in. I slept in. I forgot to fill up the water and now it’s too late. What if Vishnu comes home and there’s no water?” She started having a panic attack. Ruby hugged her friend and told her to take a few breaths to calm down.
“He’s not here yet, darling. We can fix this,” Ruby reassured her little sister. She knew full too well what triggered Lakshmi’s panic attack. Ruby checked the bathroom. “You still have a full bucket and another half there from yesterday. He won’t know the difference. That should be enough for him to freshen up. Here, get yourself ready, I’ll be back.”
Lakshmi regained her composure and hurriedly got ready. There wasn’t enough time for her to wash up, so she just brushed her teeth, cleaned her face, and changed into a fresh salwar kameez outfit. She didn’t feel great today and looked disheveled. She wanted to look her best and today just wasn’t one of those days she could get herself together. “What will Chef Vijaya think when he sees me?” was her first thought. She caught herself, embarrassed. “I’m a married woman now and can’t have unsavory thoughts like these. But what’s the harm? Vishnu doesn’t pay much attention to me anyways and when he does, he just uses me.”
Ruby had returned with supplies. She filled up another bucket in Lakshmi’s kitchen with some of her own water and she had also brought down some aloo gobi that she had made. “We help each other here, little sister,” Ruby said. Lakshmi was so grateful and
relieved. She would make it up to Ruby when Ruby needed her help.
They were about to leave for their shift, when a tired Vishnu and Dravid returned. “You haven’t left yet?” he bellowed tiredly but still angrily at Lakshmi, “Don’t piss off Mr. Iyer now. Go, go, fast you stupid woman.” Dravid backed away and quietly left to his apartment like a scared mouse. Ruby stood as a barrier between Lakshmi and Vishnu and pulled her out of the apartment safely to get to work.
Alcohol and sleep deprivation were two things that transformed Vishnu into a fiery, unreasonable devil. And he indulged in both. Alcohol by choice and sleep deprivation as a result of the job. Due to the erratic work schedule, the more sleep deprived Vishnu became, the more he drank. It was an endless cycle that was an overall bad formula for anyone in his life, including Vishnu himself.

"Holding on to anger is like grasping a hot coal with the intent of throwing it at someone else; you are the one who gets burned".
~ Buddha

Sita smiled wickedly. She took out her phone and took a quick snap of the letter and gold chain. She then texted both to Vishnu.
“Girls, girls, hurry, the performance is about to start,” they heard Guru ji call out to them in the distance. They quickly stuffed the letter and chain back into Lakshmi’s clothes and hurried back excitedly to take their roles as background dancers. They were pleased with themselves, knowing that the text would make this their last performance as extras and that tomorrow, they would replace Lakshmi in the “Gangalee” show.
~
Shri Narayana ji and his guests filed into the venue, impressed by the grandness of the decor. Tonight, the Kochi Queen Hotel offered an intimate setting with a chef’s tasting menu full of pure vegetarian India delicacies. The lighting in the room was dimmed to add to the ambiance and Indian classical music subtly played in the background.
Mr. and Mrs. Iyer were only observers tonight and not seated at the main table with the politician. They stood in the background in case they were beckoned to attend to any of Shri Narayana ji’s needs. The couple marveled at how they had transformed this once dingy venue place into a hot spot for a relatively influential local politician. They turned to one another with broad smiles, acknowledging the incredible growth they had experienced over the year and as a result of Gangalee’s arrival.
Tonight, the Kochi Queen Hotel had a full staff, which included Ruby, who were waiting tables to make sure no glass went empty, that plates were removed after each of the ten courses, and any request of a guest was satisfied. The politician was paying a hefty fee and although it didn’t cover every extra detail Mrs. Iyer thought was necessary, Mr. and Mrs. Iyer had agreed that even if they went a little bit in the red tonight, it was an investment for their future growth. If tonight’s event went flawlessly, they were assured that through word of mouth they
would have even more events to plan, transforming their hotel from a seedy motel for discrete encounters onto the map as a Kochi VIP hot spot.
After the appetizer courses had gone out and the chit chat slowed, Mrs. Iyer went to the center of stage and announced, “Gentlemen, we are very honored to have you here with us tonight. We have a very special show specially choreographed by Guru ji, who has been involved in a number of Tollywood blockbuster dance shows and has an illustrious career. We are also very pleased to introduce you all to the fabulously beautiful and talented, Gangalee, who will be performing a dance as Goddess Lakshmi herself and blessing each and every one of you in your upcoming endeavors. Gentlemen, please welcome Gangalee and the Kochi Queens.”
Mrs. Iyer exited the stage and four girls emerged. Kalika and Sita hated being side pieces in what was now Gangalee’s show, but they felt proud that tonight was going to be the last night that that bitch would have all the glory. From the stage, they could see the men crane their necks trying to get a glimpse of Gangalee. Some of the men started to get annoyed seeing only the extras on stage. The politician glared at Mr. and Mrs. Iyer. But then as the performers were dancing, the stage filled with fog and a beautiful, ethereal figure descended.
“Wow!” the men went wild, standing up now to get a better glimpse of the ethereal creature and whistling. Chef Vijaya had stepped out, along with Ruby, the boy, and the rest of the staff to catch the show. They all stood in awe of Lakshmi’s over-the-top entrance. Mr. and Mrs. Iyer smiled. They could see that the politician was overjoyed as the room filled with loud whistles and loud applause as they admired her from afar.
“She is truly a divine creature,” Ruby thought as she felt happiness for her friend. She tried to silence her jealousy as
she stood in the dark corner of the venue, further concealed in the shadow. Lakshmi danced in the spotlight on the stage with such grace and charisma. It was as if Goddess Lakshmi herself had descended from the heavens themselves to provide such a spectacular evening at the Kochi Queen Hotel. One that no one had seen there before. A blockbuster night.
~
After the show, Lakshmi was mobbed by attention from the politician and his friends. They wanted pictures with the Goddess herself, her autograph, and just any small interaction from her had them swooning. Lakshmi had a star quality about her and knew how to move through the crowd, until Mrs. Iyer came to her rescue. She herded Lakshmi back to the safety of the changing rooms to refresh and get ready to entertain at the dinner table for the remaining part of the evening.
“When she speaks, her words are like honey,” a rotund man with a terrible combover and a big mole under his nose remarked.
“Shri Narayan ji, you keep up the entertainment like this and you have my vote for your next ten campaigns,” a tall, lanky man dressed in the purest of white shirts and matching dhoti with a gold border said as he slapped the politician on the back.
“What a knockout performance! You have our support.” another compliment came from the group of men.
The politician was very pleased. He had earmarked a large part of his contributions to the entertainment bucket knowing that much of politics was the wining and dining. He pulled Mr. Iyer aside and whispered in his ear, “Once a month till my next election in six months, guaranteed. But that Gangalee must be in every show.” He then signaled to his aide who brought over a white cotton pouch full of cash. Mr. Iyer could not help himself
and smiled ear to ear and shook hands securing the deal with the politician. “Don’t forget to thank your missus,” the politician said with a wink as he left to join his guests.
Mr. Iyer quickly slipped into the changing room, where the girls were freshening up and the heavy crown that Lakshmi adorned was being taken off.
“Let her hair down tonight and add in more curls. These are traditional men. Enough skin, but not too much should be revealing on our girls,” Mrs. Iyer could be heard. Mr. Iyer grabbed her and pulled Mrs. Iyer aside as he put the pouch in both her hands. “Six more months of shows,” he told her as her eyes widened ecstatically under the weight of the money and the good news.
“Jai Lakshmi Mata! Jai Lakshmi Mata!” Mrs. Iyer chanted and held up her hands. “Girls, tonight we have been blessed. Blessed by Goddess Lakshmi herself!” She ran over to Lakshmi and embraced her so tightly. “You are the daughter I never had. Gangalee, our blessing.” Mrs. Iyer went to her office and counted the cash as the girls got ready. It was so much cash. She took twenty-five percent of the cash and put it in the safe, and then divided up the money for Guru ji, Monty, Tailor Sahib, the staff, the girls, but set aside ten percent of the remainder for her golden goose. Mrs. Iyer was a shrewd businesswoman but a fair one. Even though the Kochi Queen Hotel had been struggling, she knew she needed to keep the star happy and loyal and, in her mind, nothing spoke louder than the rupee in a big expensive city to secure that relationship.
Mrs. Iyer came out and handed everyone their share of the pie. “Keep them happy with the rest of the evening and we will see many more rewarding nights like this, girls,” Mrs. Iyer told them as their faces brightened instantly with the generous pay out that night. Everyone’s faces shone bright except Kalika and Sita’s.
The extra money was great and all, but they hadn’t come this far to be background dancers. They itched to be stars, not just at the Kochi Queen Hotel but perhaps one day, in Tollywood. They hated the attention Lakshmi was receiving in that moment and they couldn’t wait till she got home. Vishnu was going to destroy her once and for all and the thought of that sparked a new joy in their dark hearts as they joined the other girls at the tables for the rest of the evening’s entertainment.
~
As the Kochi Queens joined the guests, the entrees were just being served. A large green banana leaf was beautifully laid out in front of Lakshmi by her friend, Ruby. Both girls exchanged warm looks, Ruby quietly congratulating her friend with her eyes. The boy then approached the table with a large tray full of little tiny bowls with generous portions of various dishes. There was fluffy basmati rice in one, lentils in another, mushroom fry, vegetable stew, bread, pickles, and so many rich delicacies.
Although Lakshmi could have wiped her plate clean in under five minutes after the night’s performance, she pecked slowly at her food in an elegant fashion as she engaged in vibrant conversation with the men who tore through the meal, burping along the way with the spices bellowing out as they spoke between or through bites. In addition to training hard for the dance this evening, she had studied the latest news, politics, history, and arts with Ruby. She always knew what interested the men and realized that a small tidbit here or there could help with any awkward silences and spur conversations.
Lakshmi’s beauty, however, was always one of the hottest topics at the table. Many a times she would feel a man staring at her, unapologetically, whether at the table or from across the room. It made her feel uncomfortable at first, but over the year, she had
grown used to such attention. Sometimes a man would come up to her to just get her autograph and linger a bit too long. She had no idea what an autograph was at first but then learned to scribble something down on the piece of paper for her adoring fan. She soon developed a distinguished signature that matched her personality and charm. Lakshmi felt like a Tollywood star with all the attention that was thrown her way. The way she was adored made her feel special and worthy, as she had never felt before. Never felt in particular in Vishnu’s presence. In that thought, there was emptiness in her fans’ admiration.
Mr. and Mrs. Iyer came around the tables, staying behind the scenes and being available an earshot away in case the politician had any special requests or needs. They made their round at Lakshmi’s table with the politician profusely thanking them for blessing him and his guests with Goddess Lakshmi.
“My campaign this year is going to go so well,” the politician remarked as he turned to a religious garbed man. “Pujari sahib has analyzed my stars and it is my year to shine, Mr. Iyer.” Then, the politician got up and put his hands on Lakshmi’s shoulders. “And this is my lucky charm. Yes? Pujari sahib?”
With a big smile, the priest dressed in a dhoti and a sacred thread draped from his left shoulder to his hip, wrapped up in a beige shawl said, “Yes, Shri Narayan Ji. And tonight, you were blessed with Goddess Lakshmi herself who has come in this earthly form.” Pujari Sahib then pointed his glass of beer in Lakshmi’s direction and then took a long drink without losing his gaze over her. The table went wild with cheers as the men drank to Lakhsmi.
Mrs. Iyer came round to Lakshmi and gave her a hug, then said, “Gangalee is a true blessing. Like the daughter I never had. Even my own children haven’t brought me as much success as this divine creature.” She lifted Lakshmi’s hair and draped it across
her right shoulder to expose Lakshmi’s beautiful, swanlike neck. With the hair tucked to one side, the men caught a glimpse of her nape. Her hair cascaded down to her tightly pinned up breasts and their attention was subtly directed to Lakshmi’s cleavage. Mrs. Iyer knew that these men were the religious sort but thirsty for feminine attention. She subtlety directed their attention where it needed to be. These beautiful, young women were out of the men’s usual orbit so when the men returned home, they did so to their boring wives and families or in Pujari Sahib’s case, back to his sanctimonious duties.
All of a sudden, a loud kerfuffle could be heard in the kitchen. Pots were being thrown about and loud male voices could be heard yelling. The boy came running out of the kitchen, stew splashed all over the front of his uniform. He grabbed Mrs. Iyer, pulled her aside and frantically whispered something in her ear. Her face changed from one of contentment and pride to confusion and anger as she assessed the situation in real time. Everyone’s attention had turned away from the peaceful evening to the drama ensuing in the kitchen. When the noise didn’t die down, Mrs. Iyer swiftly grabbed her husband and ushered to one of the bigger male attendants to follow them as they proceeded into the kitchen.
Lakshmi and Ruby looked at each other and froze in terror. They were all too familiar with the other voice in the kitchen. It was an irate Vishnu. Ruby was so confused at what could have triggered him and looked at Lakshmi hoping that she knew the cause. Ruby then saw Kalika and Sita snickering together at the commotion. Even though she didn’t know what exactly had happened, Ruby was assured that those two had some hand in this.
Lakshmi was frozen. Despite the conversation that swirled around her, she felt a deafening silence. “Gangalee, Gangalee?”
one of the guests said trying to get her attention. Ruby saw this and came round immediately to help her friend.
“Sir, may I get you anything more? Or may I clear your plate?” Ruby said politely as she put her hand on Lakhsmi’s shoulder. Feeling the warm, familiar hand, Lakshmi broke from her trance and took a quick breath as if she had resurfaced from being under the water for a while. The color hadn’t quite returned to her cheeks, but her charm creeped back as she continued on with conversation amongst the male company. They were entranced by her captivating beauty that they forgot the commotion ensuing in the kitchen in that moment.
After twenty minutes or so, Mr. and Mrs. Iyer emerged from the kitchen, calm and composed as if nothing had happened. The night was almost over and everyone continued to entertain the politician and his guests. As the meal had been very heavy, Chef Vijaya had opted for a lighter, cooling dessert and out came kulfis as the final course with trays of cardamom tea.
The kulfi was playfully decorated with pistachios and rose petals. With each bite, Lakshmi tasted the rich cardamom flavor from the pods that Lakshmi had recently given Chef Vijaya. She thought to herself how talented he was, ignoring in that moment the terrible argument that had just occurred between her husband and the chef. She felt nostalgic for her time on the cardamom estate that she had worked at near her small village in the Idukki hills.
~
The evening came to a close with the politician profusely thanking Mr. and Mrs. Iyer for a spectacular evening despite the hiccup in the kitchen. “I cannot wait till next month’s
performance. The expectations are high. Very high,” the politician said as he departed the venue a very jovial man.
Once he left the hotel, the tone in the room changed instantly. The staff, including Ruby, the Kochi Queens, Guruji, Monty, Tailor Sahib and the boy from the kitchen were all there. Chef Vijaya and Vishnu were missing. Mr. Iyer stood back and Mrs. Iyer paced back and forth in front of the employees. Her face was full of disappointment and anger.
“Chef Vijaya has given his notice today and will be leaving the Kochi Queen effective immediately,” she said and then looked directly at Lakshmi, then Kalika and Sita disapprovingly. “We will find a replacement for him ASAP, so if you have any relatives or friends that you think could fill his position, come to me directly. Everyone is dismissed for the night, except Lakshmi, Kalika and Sita.” Everyone dispersed quickly signaled by Mrs. Iyer’s commanding nature.
Ruby pressed Lakshmi’s arm with reassurance and told her she would wait outside for her, but her gesture was interrupted by Mrs. Iyer, “No, Ruby. Go home. Vishnu will take his wife home.” Ruby shuddered at the thought and could feel Lakshmi’s heart sink.
Ruby exited, choosing to leave through the kitchen to check on Chef Vijaya. In the kitchen, she found a disheveled man with a pack of ice on his left eye and a darkening red blood stain that had run down his crisp white chef jacket. Ruby quickly deduced what had happened and was certain that Kalika and Sita’s jealousy had prompted their meddling.
“Are you okay?” she said, comforting Chef Vijaya.
“I will be,” Chef Vijaya said and continued, “Kalika and Sita told Vishnu about the tips.” Ruby gave Chef Vijaya a confused look with a cocked head. He continued on with his eyes looking at the
ground, “And they showed him the picture in the gold locket and note I gave her earlier this evening. I love her, Ruby. And I think she loves me back.”
Ruby smacked her forehead. “You fool. Of all the girls that come through here, why did you have to pick the married one to pursue? Now you’re out of a job and Vishnu’s going to destroy her when we get home. Where is he?”
“Mr. Iyer sent him home with Dravid and told Vishnu to calm down,” Chef Vijaya replied. “As long as my beauty…” He was interrupted as Mrs. Iyer walking in with Lakshmi in tow.
“Good, Ruby. You’re still here. Change of plans. Take Lakshmi home immediately,” she said as she yanked Lakshmi forward and put Lakshmi’s hand she was holding into Ruby’s. Ruby stood there frozen for a moment until Mrs. Iyer barked, “Go!” The girls quickly disappeared as Mrs. Iyer pulled Chef Vijaya by the ear towards her and then slapped him hard.
“All these years you were working here and she’s the only girl you decide to fool around with…” Mrs. Iyer’s voice trailed off as the girls left the kitchen and hurried home.
~
The girls stood silently on the bus home. Lakshmi could feel Ruby’s disappointment in her, but it was Ruby who was actually disappointed in herself. “Only if I had given her a firmer warning about staying away from Chef Vijaya,” Ruby thought as she looked back on Lakshmi’s flirtations with Chef Vijaya. She shook her head in dismay.
The bus ride home was nerve-racking for both Lakshmi and Ruby. It was so late at night that they had no where else to go but home. Here they knew the tyrant would be sitting in wait for them. Ruby texted Dravid to see where he was and what
Vishnu’s temperature was like, but as usual, she didn’t receive a response the entire way there. She was ticked off with Dravid, thinking, “This is so Dravid. He’s never there when you need him.”
Finally, the bus dropped Lakshmi and Ruby off and they walked to their building. They held each other’s hand tightly as they approached Apartment 6F. They both took a deep breath as Lakshmi opened the door. The suitcases and their contents had been tossed everywhere, but no one was in the living room. They looked at each other in fear as they checked the bedroom. Empty. Lakshmi went to the kitchen where she found her planter full of her lively Gangalee blossoms smashed to the ground and the Goddess Lakshmi statuette by it, smashed into pieces. Lakshmi knelt down next to the planter and picked up her broken blossoms. She let out a wail so loud, one that Ruby had never heard even when Vishnu would beat or rape her. Ruby could see the despair in Lakshmi’s eyes as she tried to console her little sister. Suddenly Ruby heard a noise in the hallway and she got up, scared that Vishnu had returned.
She shook Lakshmi and loudly whispered that she had to get up. Lakshmi was so deeply rooted on the floor and wouldn’t budge no matter how hard Ruby tried to pull her up. Ruby started panicking as she heard footsteps come closer to the apartment door. It opened and she looked up and saw Dravid meekly peeking in.
“You scared us,” Ruby exclaimed running into his arms, then punching his shoulder. “Why didn’t you respond to my text?”
“I was with Vishnu and he was mumbling about some locket and money. He was very angry so I got him drunk and gave him a sedative. He’s flat out sleeping. I finally got away. What happened?” he questioned. Ruby shook her head, sat down on
the sofa and held her head as she looked down at the floor in dismay.
“That buys us till the morning,” she replied and proceeded to tell Dravid what took place that evening as Lakshmi sat there on the kitchen floor blankly looking at her broken planter and statuette. After some time, Ruby and Dravid lifted Lakshmi up and Ruby took Lakshmi to get changed for bed. They knew Lakhsmi needed her rest for whatever tomorrow would bring with Vishnu rising from his slumber, hung over and recovering from the effects of whatever sedative Dravid had given him. Dravid cleaned up the kitchen and threw the broker planter with dirt and the broken blossoms along with the statuette into a trash bag and tied it up to be thrown out in the morning. He bid them goodnight and told Ruby to stay with Lakshmi the rest of the night.
Finally, Ruby got Lakshmi to drink some water, and on Lakshmi’s insistence, Ruby lay down beside her terrified friend in the bedroom. Lakshmi was trembling, and Ruby was too afraid to close her eyes. She just stared at the door in the darkness, fearing that it would swing open with Vishnu would come charging in and beat the daylights out of both of them.
~
Ruby was awakened by sounds in the kitchen. She must have drifted off, exhausted from the events of the prior night. She cautiously opened her eyes and looked around. She couldn’t hear any voices outside, so she got up and opened the door to the bedroom. It was just Lakshmi. Lakshmi had risen as though nothing had transpired the night before. She had filled up the buckets with water and was preparing breakfast and dinner. Ruby crept out.
“He’s not here,” Lakshmi said, noticing her friend. “Come, freshen up and have some rava kesari bhat and my special cardamom tea.” Lakhsmi had a huge smile on her face, but a coldness sat behind the feigned warmth. It felt ominous to Ruby as she got ready and joined Lakshmi for breakfast.
“I’ve made sambar for dinner to go with dosa,” Lakshmi said. “You and Dravid should join us tonight. I made plenty.” Ruby nodded and smiled hesitantly at Lakshmi. Lakshmi was chattering away as the two sat and ate the bhat, which was more delicious than any other times that Lakshmi had prepared it. It was almost time to head into work and Ruby didn’t have any fresh garments but also didn’t want to go down the hallway. Upon Lakshmi’s insistence, she borrowed a brand new, embroidered salwar kameez from her friend who had tucked this one away for some special occasion. Ruby tried to refuse the unused garments, but Lakshmi didn’t seem to let up. As she got ready in the bathroom, she heard Dravid and Vishnu come in.
“Good, Dravid is there too at least,” Ruby whispered to herself as she hurriedly changed out of her previous day’s clothes and into the fresh ones. As far as she could hear, everything was going well outside. Ruby stepped out of the bathroom with the boys seated on the sofa.
“Bring me fresh tea. Not this warmed up shit that you and that bitch have already had,” Ruby heard Vishnu bellow at Lakshmi as he pushed away the cup of tea.
“A fresh cup of cardamom tea for you too, Dravid?” Lakhsmi asked as she handed Dravid a plate of bhat. He shook his head, avoiding her gaze. “One cardamom tea coming up, Vishnu,” Lakshmi said eerily calm and cheerfully. There was something different in Lakhsmi’s voice and Ruby couldn’t quite place what. Ruby popped into the kitchen and saw Lakshmi grind up the spices and then open a drawer where she pulled out a small box.
She dropped in a few seeds into the tea spice mix in the mortar and then popped the rest into an emptied dahi container of soil she had next to her. She tossed the small box into the trash, ground up the contents in the mortar, and then threw in the freshly ground cardamom pods, spices, and the Gangalee seeds into the tea to boil.
Lakshmi noticed Ruby’s presence behind and said, “I only made fresh tea enough for Vishnu. Once this is made, let’s go to work.” Lakhsmi proceeded to water the container of soil and freshly planted seeds and set the pot on the windowsill where her planter had been. The two women stood in silence as the tea boiled. Lakhsmi added milk and sugar as Vishnu desired and handed Vishnu his cup of freshly made tea. She cleared the empty plates and signaled to Ruby that it was time for them to go. And with that, the two women left the apartment. Ruby was so relieved at how calmly the morning went and that her worst nightmare was now behind her. She overlooked the addition to the tea as perhaps Lakshmi’s secret formula to a perfect cup.
~
The bus ride to the Kochi Queen was different this morning. Lakshmi didn’t say a word but had a broad, peaceful smile brimming across her face as she looked out the barred windows and the breeze gently whispering through her hair. Ruby was not sure how to feel about Lakshmi’s reaction but after the rough night, invited the quiet morning. Everything seemed to be going so smoothly, so she let it slide. The two women arrived at the hotel, changed into their uniforms and proceeded upstairs to clean the VIP suites as they had many a times together over the past year.
It was an hour into their cleaning and the girls were working hard in Room 204. The room was fortunately unoccupied this
morning, but everything had been turned upside down after what looked like a wild after party. Again, the girls worked in total silence, not mentioning the morning or what had transpired the night before. Ruby could hear Lakshmi’s cell phone vibrate several times, but Lakshmi ignored the calls and stayed focused on stripping the bedding and scrubbing the stains left behind by last night’s guests.
Ruby couldn’t ever imagine what was going to happen next. Dravid called Ruby’s cell and started talking erratically, so frantically she couldn’t understand a thing he was saying and only got bits and pieces. She asked him to slow down and take a breath.
“Vishnu,” Dravid said. “Stopped breathing.” She asked him to repeat himself, slowly. He told her he had called Lakshmi’s phone several times but couldn’t reach her, so he called her. Ruby learned from Dravid that Vishnu had been complaining about Chef Vijaya and his dirty intentions towards Lakshmi and how he was going to teach Lakshmi a lesson tonight. But then, all of a sudden, his breathing stopped while he was driving towards the airport that morning to greet Mohan Das, a businessman visiting from Dubai. It took a moment for Dravid to assess what was going on, and luckily by the time he grasped the situation, they just missed crashing into another car on the highway, only grazing the other car. Dravid was able to push on the brake and stop the car. He had called emergency services, but it was going to take them too long to get to them, so they suggested he drive back towards the hospital.
Dravid then had to move Vishnu onto the passenger seat with the help of the other car’s driver, and he drove Vishnu as quickly as he could to Sunrise Hospital on Airport Road. Then, Dravid broke the difficult news. Vishnu hadn’t made it. He was dead.
They couldn’t revive him and he had passed away from a heart attack.
Ruby’s eyes widened as she looked across the room at a very calm Lakshmi, busily engaged in cleaning. She couldn’t believe what she was hearing and at the same time, couldn’t help but feel that Vishnu hadn’t passed away from a simple heart attack. Ruby took a step back as she pieced the morning’s events together. “Sure, he was a drunkard. Everyone knew this. But otherwise, Vishnu was in perfect health and young,” she thought as she slumped down to the floor by the foot of the bed. Lakshmi noticed and quickly came to the aid of her friend.
“What’s wrong, Ruby? Everything okay?” Lakshmi said, almost coyly. When Ruby looked up at Lakshmi blankly, Lakshmi shook her. “Ruby? Ruby, dear. What’s wrong?”
Ruby then meekly muttered as she shook her head, “Lakshmi. I’m so sorry. I’m so sorry.” Lakshmi looked at Ruby questioningly as Ruby continued on, “Vishnu passed away this morning.” Expecting her friend to burst into tears, Ruby got up and hugged her friend. Instead, Ruby was shocked, but not completely surprised, to see Lakshmi smile and then burst out into laughter.
Lakshmi then said, “I got him, Ruby. I got him. I got him before he could get us. They’ll never know that a poor, innocent, stupid girl from a small village from the outskirts of Idukki hills could do this.” Then Lakshmi looked up into the face of her friend, and pleaded, “Ruby, my dear sister, promise you won’t tell. It’ll be our little secret. We’ve killed the devil. We’ve killed the devil.” Lakshmi was laughing and jumping joyfully at the news.
Ruby was flabbergasted. She had seen Lakshmi grind the Gangalee seeds along with the other spices for the cardamom tea this morning. She suspected something was off with Lakshmi
this morning but hadn’t realized Lakshmi could be capable of something as sinister as murdering Vishnu. And Lakshmi had done so in the most surreptitious way. “There will be a police inquiry, Lakshmi. We have to be careful. No one else must know about this, otherwise we’re both going to jail,” Ruby told her friend as she trembled at the cold heartedness of Lakshmi’s actions. Yet, Ruby couldn’t help but think, “Good riddance.”
~
Chapter 7 – Sloth or Moda
“He who is not a good servant will not be a good master.”
~ Plato
Lakshmi and Ruby ran downstairs to inform Maria Ma’am of Vishnu’s passing and that they needed to somehow get to Sunrise Hospital immediately. There was no bus service that would get them there in a timely fashion and they wondered if anyone could help them out with getting a taxi. The two played helpless women, one now a widow.
Maria Ma’am was equally shocked by the news, which spread quickly across the Kochi Queen Hotel. Maria Ma’am immediately went to Mr. Iyer and Mrs. Iyer who were busy in their office managing the financial affairs and budget planning with their accountant. They both looked at each other in total disbelief. Mr. Iyer stood up and told Maria Ma’am that he’d drive the girls to the hospital. Mrs. Iyer nodded in agreement and shooed him to go quickly. The girls hopped into Mr. Iyer’s second-hand blue Mercedes and they were off to the hospital. They drove in complete silence. Ruby tried to signal to Lakshmi to cry, but Lakshmi had no tears in her to shed. She sat there, looking blankly outside the window.
“Poor girl, she’s in total shock.,” Mr. Iyer said to Ruby as he looked back at the two in the rear-view mirror of his car.  Mr. Iyer tsked as he commented on how young Vishnu was and lamented on how he was going to have to tell his parents the bad news. “I’d taken the boy on by the request of Ramesh ji. We go way back. I straightened him out best I could, but he was just one of those boys. Never was going to straighten out,” Mr. Iyer continued on, trying to break the awkwardness he felt as he made the journey to Sunrise Hospital. “So young. So young,” he trailed off, fighting off tears.
After what seemed to be the longest drive of Ruby’s life, Mr. Iyer drove into the parking lot by the Emergency Department. He told the girls he would park but that they should go in. Ruby obliged and pulled her friend out of the car and the two women made their way towards the hospital where they could see Dravid outside waving them down.
“Lakshmi. Lakshmi,” Ruby whispered as she tried to get Lakshmi’s attention. “You need to show some emotion and regret.” Lakshmi looked up at her friend, whose face was in the shadows with the sun shining brightly behind her. Lakshmi smiled at her. A smile that Ruby would never forget. A smile of pure bliss and relief.
“I’m free of that monster now. I’m free to live my life,” Lakshmi said to her friend. As they entered the hospital, they saw a frantic Dravid that quickly ran towards them, speechless as he had no idea what to say to Lakshmi. He fumbled with his words as he tried to comfort her and tried to explain that he had no idea how any of this happened.
“He was absolutely fine this morning when we left. Perhaps the sedatives I gave him last night,” Dravid mumbled. “I tried to tell the police that, but they wanted to know about the fight yesterday between Vishnu and Chef Vijaya.”
Lakshmi looked shocked. She hadn’t intended to implicate Chef Vijaya at all. She started to have a panic attack in response to that. Her breathing became irregular and heavy, until she found herself waking up on the stretcher with Ruby holding her hand.
“Poor girl. A widow at such young age,” Lakshmi woke to whisperings of the hospital staff, Ruby, Dravid and Mr. Iyer around her.
“She’s very beautiful,” a male orderly remarked.
“Yes, but second-hand now. No one will have her,” another snickered.
Lakshmi sat up on the stretcher as Ruby handed her a cup of water to drink. Then, a burly woman wearing a khaki police uniform and similarly colored hat approached.
“Lakshmi?” she questioned and Lakshmi nodded and her eyes widened at the sight of the policewoman. Lakshmi hadn’t anticipated any police involvement until Ruby mentioned it earlier. “Your husband seems to have died from natural causes, but we are running some toxicology tests to check for drugs in his system and any other anomalies.”
Lakshmi looked confusedly at Ruby. Ruby’s heart was pounding and she could feel herself begin to sweat. “Oh, Inspector ma’am is saying they have to run, eh, blood tests,” Ruby explained to Lakshmi.
“Yes,” the Inspector woman said curtly as she pounded her baton she held in one hand into the palm of the other. “The body is being transported to the coroner’s office now and Dravid has made the identification. After a few weeks, you can contact us and collect the body for funeral arrangements,” she said and then yelled to Gopal, her constable to come and give Lakshmi their card.
The inspector woman then looked Lakshmi dead in the eyes and said, “We will have a few questions at the station for you about last night. Some fight with your late husband and a Chef Vijaya,” she then clicked her fingers ushering Gopal to give them the details for the appointment at the local station in Kochi tomorrow morning.
~
After the police officers left, Lakshmi and Ruby let out deep sighs. Lakshmi hadn’t thought any of this through and Ruby started hyperventilating in response to that. Lakshmi blew some cool air in her face and shook her. “Ruby, calm down. Calm down. Everything will be okay. I know Goddess Lakshmi is with us,” Lakhsmi whispered to her friend. Lakshmi felt serenity wash over her as she realized she was no longer scared.
At that very moment, Mr. Iyer and Dravid came back from the canteen provide comfort to the grieving widow and her friend. “Girls. Eh. Lakshmi. Mrs. Iyer and I. Sorry, eh, Lakshmi for your loss. Mrs. Iyer and I will do whatever you need in the coming weeks to help you out,” Mr. Iyer stuttered as he tried to give Lakhsmi his condolences and support. Everyone knew that Vishnu was a bad-tempered alcoholic, but having taken him under his wings all these years, he saw the lad as one of his own sons. It hadn’t surprised him that the excess liquor had finally gotten the boy. After some awkward silence, Mr. Iyer left the girls and said he would head back as Dravid would take the girls home. He also promised that he wouldn’t tell Vishnu’s parents about anything until Lakshmi was ready to break the dreadful news to them, knowing that being a young widow, they would see her as unlucky and the cause of his early demise.
~
No one spoke the entire car ride home. Unbeknownst to Dravid, Lakshmi’s silence was one of relief rather than that of a bereaved widow. Lakshmi, however, was deep in thought on how she may have implicated Chef Vijaya in Vishnu’s death and how she could get around it at the police station tomorrow. She wasn’t thinking this morning when she crushed the Gangalee seeds for the special cardamom tea, which she remembered he always favored over Maa ji’s. Then it hit her. “What was she going to say to everyone back home. Her in-laws. Her parents. The village would vilify her. She was a young widow and they would all blame her for Vishnu’s death, whether it was her fault or not. To them he was a saint and had changed his ruthless ways,” she thought to herself. She turned to Goddess Lakshmi in prayer.
“Goddess Lakshmi, please help me as you have always. Please help me fulfill my dreams and save me. I took as much as I could, but I could no longer bear Vishnu’s torments. I couldn’t live like this anymore. Goddess Lakshmi, it angered me when he broke my beautiful planter and destroyed your statuette. Protect me, Goddess Lakshmi. Help me achieve my dreams here in Kochi. I do not want to go back.” Lakshmi became overwhelmed with all the possibilities. “What if the police find the poison in his system? What if they think Chef Vijaya had something to do with it because of the locket?” she thought about all the different scenarios and all the challenges up ahead. Despite the difficulties, Lakshmi still couldn’t help but feel a sense of freedom. She wasn’t scared to return back to the apartment for the first time since she moved in.
Dravid dropped Lakshmi and Ruby off at Lakshmi’s apartment and went back to his. He was very distraught seeing his friend of many years pass away right in front of him. He knew Vishnu
was no saint, but they had shared all sorts of debauchery and mischief together. Dravid couldn’t shake the feeling that Chef Vijaya had something to do with all of this, especially since it was Chef Vijaya that lost his job after being punched in the face by Vishnu yesterday.
Dravid, however, was unlikely to pursue any inquiry further than that as he also knew his friend drank terribly and something finally gave way to his reckless lifestyle. Plus, Dravid was scared that the sedative that he gave his friend may have caused the heart attack, so he reasoned it was better to stay quiet. Considering his friend’s early passing, Dravid contemplated the changes he wanted to make in his own life. He wanted to put himself on the right path and have a more stable life. He thought about cleaning up his own vices and perhaps finally starting a family with Ruby before it was too late. He thought that perhaps it was time to make her an honest woman, despite their pasts and their incompatible castes. They had been living together for years now, never putting a serious label on their relationship. Dravid felt a sense of growth and maturity in that moment.
~
In Apartment 6F, Lakshmi was seemingly too calm for Ruby’s liking. Ruby started badgering Lakshmi with a slew of questions as Lakshmi busied herself in the kitchen. Quietly as she always had been around the house, Lakshmi emptied out the spices from the pot, then washed the tea cups, plates and pot that she had used in the morning to make Vishnu’s last tea. She scrubbed the mortar and pestle whistling a sweet tune, and then bagged up the trash to take downstairs. Ruby was in disbelief at Lakshmi’s coldness and detachment from the situation.
Ruby was grateful that Vishnu was out of the picture, but never thought it would be this way. Lakshmi was a sweet, innocent girl who had lived in fear of her husband. True, he had been slowly depleting her energy and sense of security. But after Vishnu’s passing and knowing full well Lakshmi caused it, Lakshmi had lost any naiveté that Ruby had initially seen in this girl from a small village on the outskirts of Idukki Hills. She was much more complex than Ruby had initially anticipated.
When Lakshmi came back up from throwing out the trash, she proceeded to make a fresh cup of cardamom tea and finally after all the silence, Ruby saw a drop of sadness in Lakshmi’s voice as she told Ruby of one of her first encounters with Vishnu. “When his parents invited me to their house to interview me as a potential match, I remember him telling me in front of everyone that my cardamom tea surpassed even his mother’s in taste. He was so sweet and charming then.” Lakshmi then turned to look directly into Ruby’s eyes and said, “Promise me Ruby. Promise to be true to me and not tell the police what I’ve done. Only you and I know what I did, and I’ll forever be in your debt, sister.”
Ruby was glad to see some inkling of remorse in Lakshmi’s eyes in that moment because it reassured her that what Lakshmi had done was not entirely cold-hearted but the result of the nonstop abuse she had had to bear over the past year. Ruby also knew that it wasn’t just the abuse that caused Lakshmi to snap that morning, but her fear that Vishnu would inflict further pain and torture on Ruby and Chef Vijaya, not able to touch Lakshmi, Mrs. Iyer’s cash cow and his golden goose for alcohol.  Lakshmi had been thinking of her friends.
Years of living in Punjab and then in Kochi had shown Ruby that the world was a rainbow of different shades of grey. What Lakshmi had done was in itself justifiable, although not in the black-and-white letters of criminal law. She didn’t want her only
true friend to go to prison for killing a man that had no sincere appreciation for his wife and spent their marriage beating her and using her for his own gains. It made no sense to be truthful here and she promised Lakshmi then and there that she would have her back.
They sat all night discussing how they would approach the interview the next day and how to explain away the fight. They still feared that the toxicology report would trip them up in their lie, but they both felt if they stayed calm and consistent in their stories, they could possibly get away with this. At the end of the day, was the police really going to dedicate resources to an alcoholic bum like Vishnu?
~
The morning came. Ruby and Lakhsmi hadn’t slept all night and looked disheveled. They agreed that this would only add to their performance today at the station as neither felt any real grief over Vishnu’s death. They couldn’t sleep at the thought that they may be caught or that somehow Chef Vijaya could be implicated given his romantic gesture the other night.
Lakshmi made her special cardamom tea and badly joked that Ruby should not worry as this batch was not tainted. Ruby gave her a stern look, but then couldn’t help but laugh back. It helped ease the tension both of them felt. Lakshmi whipped up some sweet poora batter flavored with anise seeds. It was Ruby’s favorite and it was one of the Punjabi treats that Ruby had taught her early on in their friendship. They sat and enjoyed the tea and pancakes as the sun rose. Then, they got themselves dressed. Lakshmi had no clothes in pure white and found the most plain, dull brown cotton salwar kameez outfit she could find and Ruby found something similar. Lakshmi then held Ruby’s hand and they prayed to Goddess Lakshmi to give them
strength and protection today. Minutes into their prayers, they heard a knock on the door. It was Dravid, waiting to drive the two women to the police station for their interview.
Dravid looked run down and pitiful. He was clearly mourning the loss of his friend, both scumbags, but in relative terms, Dravid was the one with the higher moral character and was physically nonviolent. A low bar for a relationship. He knew that Vishnu was not an upstanding human. He knew that he engaged with prostitutes, drank all the hard-earned money away, lied to his family about his success in Kochi, and beat his wife. That was behavior that Dravid had grown up with and to him, it was normal. Despite knowing the ill character of this friend, Vishnu’s early death didn’t seem just. “My brother was always there for me,” he thought to himself as he reminisced about the good old bro days together. “How will I spend my days now?” Speechless and clearly distraught in his grief, Dravid ushered the two girls that it was time to head over to the police station for the interview. They hopped into the car that Vishnu once drove for the Iyers. The car still had his Aum charm that hung from the rear-view mirror that Vishnu’s mother had given him when he first came back to Kochi to find a bride. They sat in the car in complete silence as Dravid drove to the police station.
~
They arrived promptly at 8:45am, well ahead of the 9am interview time. They inquired around the station until they finally found where they needed to be. Lakshmi and Ruby sat on a bench outside Inspector Radikha Nair’s office. Dravid had to return to work and told them to text him when they were ready to head back and he’d come pick them up.
Ruby noticed how helpful Dravid was being. He had never taken any responsibility and today he was so attentive to their every
need. She knew the typical pattern of the males in her life. This event had sparked a feeling of responsibility in Dravid, but she knew it was a temporary phase of grief, although it inspired a small bit of hope in her.
Men in her life were never going to make an honest woman out of her. She saw the yearning in his eyes to change his life and to settle down, but she also knew that he had always been too afraid of any commitment and when things slowed down, he would run as he always did. Besides, she was quite content with the arrangement they had, but realized she wanted more for her life now. Although Dravid had never been abusive like Vishnu, he was just as immature and he was holding her back. She appreciated the freedom to be able to leave him whenever she chose to do so, without any whiplash from family or society, having abandoned those ideals when she left Punjab.
As they waited, Ruby’s thoughts turned to worry for Lakshmi as they sat and waited outside the Inspector woman’s office. The wait turned from a half past 9 to 10 am. Lakshmi asked the warden patrolling the corridors in the station when they’d be seen.
“What’s your hurry, pretty lady. She’s a busy woman. She is dealing with some murder case right now,” the short grouchy female warden barked at them.
Lakshmi and Ruby looked at each other and their eyes widened in shock. They immediately started feeling nervous thinking that the murder case that the warden was speaking about was that of Vishnu’s. Ruby cupped her hand around Lakshmi’s ear and whispered, “They are suspicious of us and are keeping us waiting, Lakshmi. What do we do?” Lakshmi took Ruby’s hand in hers and looked at her reassuringly and with her eyes, Lakshmi indicated her to stay quiet and calm. “With Goddess
Lakshmi’s help, everything will be okay, my dear sister,” Lakshmi said gently. “All will be well.
It was nearly noon when Gopal barged through the office doors and told the girls to come into the office. Inspector Radikha Nair was seated on a brown, worn leather office chair behind a large solid oak desk and was wrapping up a phone call. She pointed to the women to take the two seats in front of her desk. She covered the speaker on the phone and asked, “Thums Up, Limca? Tea? Coffee?” The girls shook their heads but the Inspector woman instructed Gopal to bring her a piping hot cup of Madras coffee to help her cool down on this sweltering summer day.
“Thank you for coming in. This inquiry is a formality only that we have to do in these situations. We are very sorry ma’am for having to drag you in here, but we have a few questions to clear up for the report as we wait on the autopsy results,” the Inspector woman said. She continued, “We understand that your husband was okay in the morning and had a heart attack on the way to the airport yesterday. Did he have any health issues?”
Lakshmi shook her head, “No ma’am. He drank a lot of alcohol, but that’s what men do.”
“Was he seeing a doctor?” the Inspector asked looking Lakshmi squarely in the eyes.
“No ma’am. We don’t have time. We are working all the time and are very poor,” Lakshmi said. The Inspector woman nodded her head, having seen the worst of poverty during her decade of service. Ruby was impressed by Lakshmi’s cool as the questioning continued.
